h_{\max}\gg h_{\beta,L_{*}}
and the vertical increment
V
is much smaller than its maximal value
L_{*}(b-a+2h_{\max})^{\beta-1}
, as is typically the case in our applications of Proposition
13
. In particular, when
\varepsilon=1/n
, the first of the aforementioned terms in (
23
) yields a strictly faster rate
n^{-\beta/(2\beta+1)}
than that in (
24
) for all
\beta\in[1,2)
. For instance, when
\beta=1
and
b-a
,
L_{*}=V
and
h_{\max}
are all regarded as universal constants, the bound in (
23
) scales as
n^{-1/3}
while that in (
24
) is of constant order.

Proof.

First, we show that
\Gamma(x)\leq\Gamma_{\max}
for all
x\in[a,b]
. Indeed, since
g
is decreasing,

\Gamma(x)\leq\xi_{x}(h_{\max})^{2}\leq V^{2}\vee\frac{\varepsilon}{h_{\max}^{3}}.

Moreover, because
(L_{*}h^{\beta-1})^{2}\leq\varepsilon/h^{3}
for all
h\in(0,h_{\beta,L_{*}}]
, it follows from the Hölder condition that

\Gamma(x)\leq\xi_{x}(h_{\min})^{2}\leq(L_{*}h_{\min}^{\beta-1})^{2}\vee\frac{\varepsilon}{h_{\min}^{3}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{h_{\min}^{3}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{h_{\beta,L_{*}}^{3}}\vee\frac{\varepsilon}{h_{\max}^{3}},

so taking the minimum of these bounds yields
\Gamma(x)\leq\Gamma_{\max}
, as required.

To verify that
\Gamma\colon[a,b]\to[0,\Gamma_{\max}]
is Borel measurable,
we define
\mathcal{H}:=\bigl((h_{\min},h_{\max}]\cap\mathbb{Q}\bigr)\cup\{h_{\min}\}
and show that

\Gamma(x)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\xi_{x}(h)^{2}

(25)

for every
x\in[a,b]
. Indeed, given
\eta>0
, we can find
h^{\prime}\in[h_{\min},h_{\max}]
such that
\xi_{x}(h^{\prime})^{2}<\Gamma(x)+\eta
. Since
h\mapsto\omega\bigl(g(x)-g(x+\omega h)\bigr)
is increasing for
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, since
h\mapsto(\varepsilon/h^{3})^{1/2}
is continuous, and since
\mathcal{H}
is dense in
[h_{\min},h_{\max}]
, there exists
h^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
with
h^{\prime\prime}\leq h^{\prime}
such that
\xi_{x}(h^{\prime\prime})^{2}<\Gamma(x)+\eta
. Since
\eta
was arbitrary, this establishes (
25
). Thus,
x\mapsto\Gamma(x)
is a countable pointwise infimum of Borel measurable functions
x\mapsto\xi_{x}(h)
over
h\in\mathcal{H}
, and hence is Borel measurable.

To derive the bound (
22
) on the Lebesgue measure of
\mathcal{S}_{\zeta}:=\{x\in[a,b]:\Gamma(x)>\zeta\}
for
\zeta\in[0,\Gamma_{\max}]
, first consider
\zeta\geq\varepsilon/h_{\max}^{3}
. Since
\Gamma_{\max}\leq\varepsilon/h_{\min}^{3}
, we have
h_{\zeta}:=(\varepsilon/\zeta)^{1/3}\in[h_{\min},h_{\max}]
, so if
\max_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\omega\bigl(g(x)-g(x+\omega h_{\zeta})\bigr)\leq\sqrt{\zeta}
, then
\Gamma(x)\leq\xi_{x}(h_{\zeta})^{2}\leq\zeta
. Thus, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(\zeta):=\bigl\{x\in[a-h_{\max},b+h_{\max}-h_{\zeta}]:g(x)-g(x+h_{\zeta})>\sqrt{\zeta}\bigr\},

each
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\zeta}
satisfies either
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}(\zeta)
or
x^{\prime}-h_{\zeta}\in\mathcal{X}(\zeta)
, i.e.
\mathcal{S}_{\zeta}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{X}(\zeta)}\{x,x+h_{\zeta}\}
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)
be a maximal
h_{\zeta}
-separated subset of
\mathcal{X}(\zeta)
, so that any distinct
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)
satisfy
|x-x^{\prime}|>h_{\zeta}
. Then
\mathcal{X}(\zeta)\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)}[x-h_{\zeta},x+h_{\zeta}]
,
and

\displaystyle\sqrt{\zeta}|\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)|<\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)}\bigl(g(x)-g(x+h_{\zeta})\bigr)\leq g(a-h_{\max})-g(b+h_{\max})=V,

since
g
is decreasing. Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\zeta}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{X}(\zeta)}\{x,x+h_{\zeta}\}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)}[x-h_{\zeta},x+2h_{\zeta}],

so
\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{\zeta})\leq 3h_{\zeta}|\mathcal{X}_{\circ}(\zeta)|<3h_{\zeta}V/\sqrt{\zeta}=3V\varepsilon^{1/3}/\zeta^{5/6}
for
\zeta\in[\varepsilon/h_{\max}^{3},\Gamma_{\max}]
. Moreover,
\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{\zeta})\leq b-a
trivially for all
\zeta\in[0,\Gamma_{\max}]
.
This yields (
22
), and hence by Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\Gamma(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{a}^{b}\int_{0}^{\Gamma_{\max}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Gamma(x)>\zeta\}}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\Gamma_{\max}}\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{\zeta})\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\leq\frac{(b-a)\varepsilon}{h_{\max}^{3}}+\int_{\varepsilon/h_{\max}^{3}}^{\Gamma_{\max}}\frac{3V\varepsilon^{1/3}}{\zeta^{5/6}}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(b-a)\varepsilon}{h_{\max}^{3}}+18V\varepsilon^{1/3}\Gamma_{\max}^{1/6}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Gamma_{\max}>\varepsilon/h_{\max}^{3}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(b-a)\varepsilon}{h_{\max}^{3}}+18V\bigl\{(V\varepsilon)^{1/3}\wedge(L_{*}\varepsilon^{\beta})^{1/(2\beta+1)}\bigr\},

where the final inequality follows from the definition of
\Gamma_{\max}
. Therefore, (
23
) holds.
∎

By
9
, a Lebesgue density
f_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}
is log-concave if and only if the corresponding
density quantile function

J_{0}:=f_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}

(
56
)
is concave on
[0,1]
, where
F_{0}
and
F_{0}^{-1}\colon[0,1]\to[-\infty,\infty]
denote the corresponding distribution and quantile functions respectively. We will use the following elementary facts
(e.g.
32
, Remark 3 and Lemma 17)
.

Lemma 14
.

If
f_{0}
is an absolutely continuous Lebesgue density on
\mathbb{R}
with score function
\psi_{0}
, then

(a)

J_{0}(0)=J_{0}(1)=0
and
J_{0}
is absolutely continuous with
J_{0}^{\prime}=\psi_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
Lebesgue almost everywhere on
(0,1)
.

(b)

\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}=\int_{F_{0}(x)}^{F_{0}(x^{\prime})}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}
for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.

(c)

F_{0}^{-1}(v)-F_{0}^{-1}(u)=\int_{u}^{v}1/J_{0}
for
u,v\in(0,1)
.

The proofs of our upper bounds rely on applying Proposition
13
on subintervals
\{[a_{j},a_{j+1}]:j\in\mathbb{Z}\}
defined as follows.
Given a continuous log-concave density
f_{0}
, we henceforth take
\psi_{0}=\phi_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}
and
J_{0}^{\prime}\equiv J_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}=\psi_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
for concreteness, although our analysis does not depend on any particular version of the score function provided that it is decreasing. For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, define

u_{j}:=\begin{cases}2^{j-1}\quad&\text{if }j\leq 0\\
1-2^{-(j+1)}\quad&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\qquad\quad a_{j}:=F_{0}^{-1}(u_{j}),

so that
u_{j}\wedge(1-u_{j})=2^{-|j|-1}
, and by the definition of
J_{0}
and Lemma
14
(a)
,

F_{0}(a_{j})=u_{j},\quad f_{0}(a_{j})=J_{0}(u_{j})\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{0}(a_{j})=J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j}).

The sequence
(a_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
has the following key properties.

Lemma 15
.

For any continuous log-concave density
f_{0}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}(a_{j})|=|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{J_{0}(u_{j})}{u_{j}\wedge(1-u_{j})}=2^{|j|+1}f_{0}(a_{j}),

(26)

\displaystyle\sup_{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]}\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}\in[u_{j},u_{j+1}]}\frac{J_{0}(u^{\prime})}{J_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}\leq 2,

(27)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{j}-a_{j-1}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq a_{j+1}-a_{j}\leq 8(a_{j}-a_{j-1}),

(28)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-(|j|+3)}}{f_{0}(a_{j})}\leq a_{j+1}-a_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{-|j|}}{f_{0}(a_{j})}\wedge\frac{2^{-|j+1|}}{f_{0}(a_{j+1})}\leq\frac{2}{|\psi_{0}(a_{j})|\vee|\psi_{0}(a_{j+1})|}.

(29)

Proof.

Both (
26
) and (
27
) rely on the fact that
J_{0}
is concave
(
9
, Proposition A.1(c))
, with
J_{0}(0)=J_{0}(1)=0
by Lemma
14
(a)
. It follows that
0=J_{0}(0)\leq J_{0}(u)-uJ_{0}^{\prime}(u)
and
0=J_{0}(1)\leq J_{0}(u)+(1-u)J_{0}^{\prime}(u)
for
u\in(0,1)
, so

|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|=\max\{J_{0}^{\prime}(u),-J_{0}^{\prime}(u)\}\leq\frac{J_{0}(u)}{u}\vee\frac{J_{0}(u)}{1-u},

and (
26
) holds. Now fix
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and any
x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
such that
x^{\prime}\leq x^{\prime\prime}
. Then
u^{\prime}:=F_{0}(x^{\prime})
and
u^{\prime\prime}:=F_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})
satisfy
u^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime\prime}\leq u_{j+1}\leq 2u_{j}\leq 2u^{\prime}
and
1-u^{\prime\prime}\leq 1-u^{\prime}\leq 1-u_{j}\leq 2(1-u_{j+1})\leq 2(1-u^{\prime\prime})
,
so

\displaystyle\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{u^{\prime}}=\frac{J_{0}(u^{\prime})}{u^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{J_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}{u^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}{u^{\prime\prime}}\geq\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}{2u^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}{1-u^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{J_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})}{1-u^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{J_{0}(u^{\prime})}{1-u^{\prime}}=\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{1-u^{\prime}}\geq\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{2(1-u^{\prime\prime})}.

Thus,
1/2\leq f_{0}(x^{\prime})/f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})=J_{0}(u^{\prime})/J_{0}(u^{\prime\prime})\leq 2
, which establishes (
27
). Consequently,
1/2\leq J_{0}(u)/J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2
for all
u\in[u_{j-1},u_{j+1}]
. Moreover,

u_{j+1}-u_{j}=2^{j-1}
for
j\leq-1
and
u_{j+1}-u_{j}=2^{-(j+2)}
for
j\geq 0
, so
1/2\leq(u_{j+1}-u_{j})/(u_{j}-u_{j-1})\leq 2
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence by Lemma
14
(c)
,

\displaystyle a_{j}-a_{j-1}

\displaystyle=\int_{u_{j-1}}^{u_{j}}\frac{1}{J_{0}}\leq\frac{2(u_{j}-u_{j-1})}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq\frac{4(u_{j+1}-u_{j})}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq\int_{u_{j}}^{u_{j+1}}\frac{8}{J_{0}}=8(a_{j+1}-a_{j}),

(30)

\displaystyle a_{j+1}-a_{j}

\displaystyle=\int_{u_{j}}^{u_{j+1}}\frac{1}{J_{0}}\leq\frac{2(u_{j+1}-u_{j})}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq\frac{4(u_{j}-u_{j-1})}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq\int_{u_{j-1}}^{u_{j}}\frac{8}{J_{0}}=8(a_{j}-a_{j-1}),

(31)

which yields (
28
). Moreover, by (
27
),
J_{0}(u_{j+1})/2\leq J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2J_{0}(u_{j+1})
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, by applying the final inequality in (
30
), followed by the first inequality in (
31
) (when
j\leq-1
) and the first inequality in (
30
) with a shifted index (when
j\geq 0
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-(|j|+3)}}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq\frac{u_{j+1}-u_{j}}{2J_{0}(u_{j})}\leq a_{j+1}-a_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\dfrac{2(u_{j+1}-u_{j})}{J_{0}(u_{j})}=\dfrac{2^{-j}}{J_{0}(u_{j})}&\text{if }j\leq-1\\[10.0pt]
\dfrac{2(u_{j+1}-u_{j})}{J_{0}(u_{j+1})}=\dfrac{2^{-(j+1)}}{J_{0}(u_{j+1})}&\text{if }j\geq 0\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2^{-|j|}}{J_{0}(u_{j})}\wedge\dfrac{2^{-|j+1|}}{J_{0}(u_{j+1})}\leq\frac{2}{|\psi_{0}(a_{j})|}\wedge\frac{2}{|\psi_{0}(a_{j+1})|},

where the final bound follows from (
26
). This completes the proof of (
29
).
∎

Since
J_{0}^{\prime}
is decreasing, it turns out that the integrals
\int_{u_{j}}^{u_{k}}J_{0}^{\prime}
and
\int_{u_{j}}^{u_{k}}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}
for
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}
are always within a universal constant factor of their Riemann sum approximations with respect to the grid
(u_{\ell})_{\ell=j}^{k}
. We will use the following two explicit bounds to incorporate constraints on the Fisher information in the proofs of our main results.

Lemma 16
.

For
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
j<k
, we have

\displaystyle|J_{0}(u_{j})-J_{0}(u_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=j}^{k}2^{-|\ell|}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|,

(32)

\displaystyle i(f_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-|\ell|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}.

(33)

Proof.

Since
2^{-(|\ell|+2)}\leq u_{\ell}-u_{\ell-1}\leq 2^{-(|\ell|+1)}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
J_{0}^{\prime}
is decreasing on
(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle|J_{0}(u_{j})-J_{0}(u_{k})|\leq\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{k}\biggl|\int_{u_{\ell-1}}^{u_{\ell}}J_{0}^{\prime}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{k}(u_{\ell}-u_{\ell-1})\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|\bigr)\leq\sum_{\ell=j}^{k}2^{-|\ell|}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|,

so (
32
) holds. Next,
J_{0}=f_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
is concave with
\sup_{u\in(0,1)}J_{0}(u)>0
, and
J_{0}(0)=J_{0}(1)=0
by Lemma
14
(a)
, so
\lim_{u\searrow 0}J_{0}^{\prime}(u)>0>\lim_{u\nearrow 1}J_{0}^{\prime}(u)
and hence
m:=\max\{j\in\mathbb{Z}:J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j})>0\}
is finite. Since
J_{0}^{\prime}
is decreasing, for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\ell\neq m+1
, we have
|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|\geq|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell-1})|\wedge|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|
for all
u\in(u_{\ell-1},u_{\ell})
. Therefore, by Lemma
14
(b)
,

\displaystyle i(f_{0})=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{u_{\ell-1}}^{u_{\ell}}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\neq m+1}2^{-(|\ell|+2)}\bigl(J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell-1})^{2}\wedge J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{m}2^{-|\ell|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell=m+2}^{\infty}2^{-|\ell|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{m}2^{-|\ell|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell=m+1}^{\infty}2^{-|\ell+1|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-|\ell|}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2},

which yields (
33
).
∎

Recall from (
18
) the definition of our multiscale estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}
. The following lemma is a central plank of our proof strategy. The high-probability bound (
36
) facilitates the application of (
23
) in Proposition
13

by simplifying the pointwise inequality in Proposition
12
(b)
. Lemma
17
(b)
serves a dual purpose: first, it is used to verify in Lemma
17
(c)
and later (
39
) that the restrictions on
h
in (
36
) are compatible with those in the definition of
\Gamma
in Proposition
13
; second, we will bound
V_{j}
uniformly over the subclasses of log-concave densities
f_{0}
in the following sections, and then sum the resulting contributions from (
23
) over
j\in\mathbb{Z}
to obtain our main results.

Lemma 17
.

For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\kappa_{j}:=(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/8
and define the function
g_{j}\colon[a_{j}-\kappa_{j},a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j}]\to\mathbb{R}
by

g_{j}(x):=\psi_{0}(x)f_{0}(a_{j})^{1/2}.

(34)

(a)

Let
n\geq 3
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then there exists a universal constant
C_{0}>0
such that on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
of probability at least
1-\delta
, we have for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
that

\displaystyle\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)-\psi_{0}(x)\bigr)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

(35)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\inf\biggl\{\bigl(g_{j}(x-\omega h)-g_{j}(x)\bigr)^{2}\vee\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{h^{3}}:\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq h\leq\kappa_{j}\biggr\}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(36)

(b)

Let
V_{j}:=g_{j}(a_{j}-\kappa_{j})-g_{j}(a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j})
. Then

V_{j}^{2}\leq\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|+|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2^{2|j|+10}J_{0}(u_{j+1})^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2^{2|j|+12}J_{0}(u_{j})^{3}.

(c)

If
2^{-|j|}\geq 2^{13/2}C_{0}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}
,
then
C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}/f_{0}(x)\leq\kappa_{j}
, so the integrand in (
36
) is finite for all
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
.

Proof.

(a)
The initial bound (
35
) follows directly from Proposition
12
(b)
. Next, for fixed
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, we seek
(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)
. Consider
z=x-\omega h
and
h\in(0,\kappa_{j}/2]
. By (
28
) in Lemma
15
,

\kappa_{j}=\frac{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}{8}\leq(a_{j}-a_{j-1})\wedge(a_{j+2}-a_{j+1}),

(37)

so for any
(z,h)
as above, we either have
[z-h,z+h]=[x-2\omega h,x]\subseteq[a_{j-1},a_{j+1}]
or
[x-2\omega h,x]\subseteq[a_{j},a_{j+2}]
. Since
K_{h}(\cdot)
and
\mathbbm{1}_{[-h,h]}/(2h)
are densities supported on
[-h,h]
, both

\frac{P_{0}([z-h,z+h])}{2h}=\frac{1}{2h}\int_{-h}^{h}f_{0}(x-\omega h-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\quad\;\text{and}\quad\;f_{h}(z)=\int_{-h}^{h}f_{0}(x-\omega h-y)K_{h}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y

lie in
\bigl[\inf_{x^{\prime}\in[x-2\omega h,x]}f_{0}(x^{\prime}),\sup_{x^{\prime}\in[x-2\omega h,x]}f_{0}(x^{\prime})\bigr]
. Thus, by (
27
) in Lemma
15
,

\displaystyle\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{h}(z)}\leq\frac{f_{0}(x)}{\inf_{x^{\prime}\in[x-2\omega h,x]}f_{0}(x^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in[x-2\omega h,x]}\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}\leq\max_{k\in\{j,j+1\}}\sup_{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in[a_{k-1},a_{k+1}]}\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}\leq 4,

\displaystyle\max_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{P_{0}([z,z+\omega h])}{hf_{h}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{P_{0}([z-h,z+h])}{hf_{h}(z)}\leq 2\sup_{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in[x-2\omega h,x]}\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime})}{f_{0}(x^{\prime\prime})}\leq 8,

so

\displaystyle\Pi_{1,z,h}

\displaystyle=138+35\biggl(\frac{P_{0}([z-h,z+h])\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{\{hf_{h}(z)\}\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}}\biggr)^{1/2}\leq 138+35\times 2^{3/2}=:C_{1},

\displaystyle\Pi_{2,z,h}

\displaystyle=374\max_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\biggl(\frac{P_{0}([z,z+\omega h])\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{\{hf_{h}(z)\}\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}}\biggr)^{1/2}\leq 374\times 2^{3/2}

are bounded above by universal constants. Moreover,
\psi_{0}
is decreasing and
[z-h,z+h]\subseteq[a_{j-1},a_{j+2}]
, so by Lemma
10
together with (
29
) in Lemma
15
and (
37
),

\displaystyle|\psi_{h}(z)|\leq|\psi_{0}(z-h)|\vee|\psi_{0}(z+h)|

\displaystyle\leq|\psi_{0}(a_{j-1})|\vee|\psi_{0}(a_{j+2})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k\in\{j-1,j+1\}}\frac{2}{a_{k+1}-a_{k}}\leq\frac{2}{\kappa_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{h}.

Thus, if
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
and

\frac{16C_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq h\leq\frac{\kappa_{j}}{2},

then
z=x-\omega h
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{1}{4C_{1}^{2}}<1,\qquad\alpha_{z,h}\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\leq\frac{1}{4},

\displaystyle\beta_{z,h}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\times 374\times 2^{3/2}}{h}\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{h^{3}f_{0}(x)}\Bigr)^{1/2}.

Hence
(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)
, and

\displaystyle\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\lesssim\inf\biggl\{\bigl(\psi_{0}(x-2\omega h)-\psi_{0}(x)\bigr)^{2}f_{0}(x)+\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{h^{3}}:\frac{16C_{1}^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq h\leq\frac{\kappa_{j}}{2}\biggr\}

for
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
. Therefore, by integrating over
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
, (
36
) holds with
C_{0}=32C_{1}^{2}
.

(b)
By (
27
) in Lemma
15
,
f_{0}(x)\leq 2f_{0}(a_{j})
for all
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
. Moreover, since
[a_{j}-\kappa_{j},a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j}]\subseteq[a_{j-1},a_{j+2}]
by (
37
),

\displaystyle V_{j}:=g_{j}(a_{j}-\kappa_{j})-g_{j}(a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\psi_{0}(a_{j-1})-\psi_{0}(a_{j+2})\bigr)f_{0}(a_{j})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\bigl(J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})-J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})\bigr)J_{0}(u_{j})^{1/2}.

Together with (
26
) and (
27
) in Lemma
15
, this yields

\displaystyle V_{j}^{2}\leq\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|+|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(2^{|j-1|+1}J_{0}(u_{j-1})+2^{|j+2|+1}J_{0}(u_{j+2})\bigr)^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})

\displaystyle\leq(2\times 2^{|j|+4})^{2}J_{0}(u_{j+1})^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2^{2|j|+12}J_{0}(u_{j})^{3}.

(c)
If
2^{-|j|}\geq 2^{13/2}C_{0}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}
, then by (
27
) and (
29
) in Lemma
15
, every
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
satisfies

\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{2C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(a_{j})}\leq 2^{|j|+4}C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}(a_{j+1}-a_{j})\leq\frac{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}{2^{5/2}C_{0}^{1/2}}<\frac{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}{8}=\kappa_{j}.\qed

A.1.1
Proofs for Section
2.2

To gain further insight into the dependence on
L,r
in the conclusions (
8
) and (
9
) of Theorem
3
, and motivate the choice of the index
j_{\star}
in two crucial bounds (
45
) and (
46
) in the following proof, the following elementary considerations provide some helpful parallels. Given a non-negative sequence
(b_{j})
such that
b_{j}\leq L
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
(corresponding to
|\psi_{0}|
being bounded uniformly by
L
when
\gamma=1
), we have

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-2j/3}b_{j}^{2}\leq L^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-2j/3}=\frac{L^{2}}{2^{2/3}-1}.

Suppose that we impose the additional restriction
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j}b_{j}^{2}\leq r
, which is analogous to the condition
i(f_{0})\leq r
via (
33
). First, we can replace
r
with
r^{\prime}:=r\wedge L^{2}
since
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j}b_{j}^{2}\leq L^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j}=L^{2}
a priori. Then to bound the left-hand side of the display above, we can define
j^{*}:=\lfloor\log_{2}(L^{2}/r^{\prime})\rfloor+1
, so that

\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-2j/3}b_{j}^{2}\leq 2^{j^{*}/3}\sum_{j=1}^{j^{*}}2^{-j}b_{j}^{2}+L^{2}\sum_{j=j^{*}+1}^{\infty}2^{-2j/3}\leq 2^{j^{*}/3}r^{\prime}+\frac{2^{-2j^{*}/3}L^{2}}{2^{2/3}-1}\lesssim r^{\prime}\frac{L^{2/3}}{r^{\prime 1/3}},

(38)

where the choice of
j^{*}
balances the two terms in the penultimate inequality.
To see that this bound is tight up to a multiplicative universal constant, consider the sequence
(b_{j})
with
b_{j}=0
for
j\in[j^{*}]
and
b_{j}=L
for
j\geq j^{*}+1
. The right-hand side of (
38
) is akin to the multiplicative factor in front of
\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}
on the right-hand side of (
8
) when
\gamma=1
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Fix
f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}
and let
j_{n,\delta}:=\big\lfloor\log_{2}(2^{-13/2}C_{0}^{-3/2}/\varepsilon_{n,\delta})\big\rfloor_{+}
, where
C_{0}
is the universal constant in Lemma
17
(a)
. If
j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j_{n,\delta}-1\}
,
then necessarily
j_{n,\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
and
2^{-|j|}\geq 2^{13/2}C_{0}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}
. By Lemma
17
(c)
,
C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}/f_{0}(x)\leq\kappa_{j}
, so the infimum in (
36
) is taken over a non-empty set of bandwidths
h
for every
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
. Moreover, by (
27
) in Lemma
15
and Lemma
17
(b)
,

\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{2C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{\bigl(f_{0}(a_{j+1})^{2}f_{0}(a_{j})\bigr)^{1/3}}\leq\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{2^{2|j|+10}J_{0}(u_{j+1})^{2}J_{0}(u_{j})}\Bigr)^{1/3}\leq\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{V_{j}^{2}}\Bigr)^{1/3}.

(39)

We may therefore apply Proposition
13
to the function
g\equiv g_{j}
in (
34
), with
\beta=1
,
L_{*}=V\equiv V_{j}
,
\varepsilon\equiv\varepsilon_{n,\delta}
,
h_{\max}\equiv\kappa_{j}
, and deduce from Lemma
17
(a,b)
and (
29
) in Lemma
15
that

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\inf\Bigl\{\bigl(g_{j}(x)-g_{j}(x-\omega h)\bigr)^{2}\vee\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{h^{3}}:\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{V_{j}^{2}}\Bigr)^{1/3}\wedge\kappa_{j}\leq h\leq\kappa_{j}\Bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim V_{j}^{4/3}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\frac{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{\kappa_{j}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2/3}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+2^{2(|j|+3)}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}.

(40)

Next, since
J_{0}(0)=J_{0}(1)=0
by Lemma
14
(a)
,

|J_{0}(u)|\leq\int_{0}^{u}|J_{0}^{\prime}|\wedge\int_{u}^{1}|J_{0}^{\prime}|\leq L\int_{0}^{u\wedge(1-u)}v^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\,\mathrm{d}v=2L\,\frac{u^{(1+\gamma)/2}\wedge(1-u)^{(1+\gamma)/2}}{1+\gamma}

for
u\in[0,1]
, so

f_{0}(a_{j})=J_{0}(u_{j})\leq 2L\,\frac{\{u_{j}\wedge(1-u_{j})\}^{(1+\gamma)/2}}{1+\gamma}=\frac{2^{1-(|j|+1)(1+\gamma)/2}L}{1+\gamma}

(41)

for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. We will now bound (
40
) in two different ways depending on the value of
j
. First, recalling from (
7
) that
i(f_{0})\leq\min\{r,2^{1-\gamma}L^{2}/\gamma\}=:\tilde{r}
, let
j_{\star}:=\min\{j\in\mathbb{N}:2^{j}\geq(L^{2}/\tilde{r})^{1/\gamma}\}
. If
j\in\{0,\dotsc,j_{\star}\}
, then by (
32
), (
41
), the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
33
) in Lemma
16
,

\displaystyle 2^{j/2}J_{0}(u_{j})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j/2}J_{0}(u_{j_{\star}})+\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{\star}}2^{(j-2\ell)/2}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|\lesssim_{\gamma}2^{j/2}2^{-\frac{j_{\star}(1+\gamma)}{2}}L+\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{\star}}2^{-(\ell-j)/2}\cdot 2^{-\ell/2}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j_{\star}\gamma/2}L+\biggl(\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{\star}}2^{-(\ell-j)}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{\star}}2^{-\ell}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\lesssim\frac{\tilde{r}^{1/2}}{L}L+i(f_{0})^{1/2}\lesssim\tilde{r}^{1/2}.

(42)

Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality and (
33
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{j_{\star}}2^{-j/3}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}

\displaystyle=2^{j_{\star}/3}\sum_{j=0}^{j_{\star}}2^{-(j_{\star}-j)/3}\cdot 2^{-2j/3}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\biggl(\sum_{j=0}^{j_{\star}}2^{-(j_{\star}-j)}\biggr)^{1/3}\biggl(\sum_{j=-1}^{j_{\star}+2}2^{-j}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j})^{2}\biggr)^{2/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\tilde{r}^{2/3}.

(43)

Similarly, for
j\in\{-j_{\star},\dotsc,0\}
, we have

2^{|j|/2}J_{0}(u_{j})\lesssim\tilde{r}^{1/2},\qquad\sum_{j=-j_{\star}}^{0}2^{-|j|/3}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\tilde{r}^{2/3}.

(44)

Thus, letting
j^{\prime}:=j_{\star}\wedge j_{n,\delta}
, we deduce from (
40
)–(
44
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{-j^{\prime}}}^{a_{j^{\prime}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim_{\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}\sum_{j=-j^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}-1}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2/3}+L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\sum_{j=-j^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}-1}2^{|j|(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim\tilde{r}^{1/3}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}\sum_{j=-j_{\star}}^{j_{\star}}2^{-|j|/3}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)^{4/3}+2^{j_{\star}(1-\gamma)}L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim\tilde{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\tilde{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}.

(45)

For our second bound, it follows from (
40
), Lemma
17
(b)
and (
41
) that if
j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j_{n,\delta}-1\}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}2^{4|j|/3}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+2^{2|j|}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}L^{2}\bigl(2^{|j|(\frac{1}{3}-\gamma)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+2^{|j|(1-\gamma)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr).

Therefore, if
j_{\star}<j_{n,\delta}
, in which case
j_{n,\delta}\geq 1
and
\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\asymp 2^{-j_{n,\delta}}<2^{-j_{\star}}\asymp_{\gamma}(\tilde{r}/L^{2})^{1/\gamma}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j_{\star}}}^{a_{j_{n,\delta}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{\gamma}L^{2}\sum_{j=j_{\star}}^{j_{n,\delta}-1}\bigl(2^{j(\frac{1}{3}-\gamma)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+2^{j(1-\gamma)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr)

(46)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\lesssim_{\gamma}M:=\begin{cases}L^{2}\bigl(2^{j_{n,\delta}(\frac{1}{3}-\gamma)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma}\bigr)\asymp L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma}\;\;&\text{if }\gamma\in(0,1/3)\\[8.0pt]
L^{2}\bigl(j_{n,\delta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}\bigr)\asymp L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}\log_{+}\Bigl(\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr)\;\;&\text{if }\gamma=1/3\\[10.0pt]
L^{2}\bigl(2^{-j_{\star}(\gamma-\frac{1}{3})}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma}\bigr)\asymp_{\gamma}\tilde{r}\Bigl(\dfrac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}\;\;&\text{if }\gamma\in(1/3,1].\end{cases}

Similarly,
\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{-j_{n,\delta}}}^{a_{-j_{\star}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{\gamma}M
.
Further, by (
6
) and Lemma
14
(b)
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{a_{-j_{n,\delta}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{u_{-j_{n,\delta}}}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{2^{-(j_{n,\delta}+1)}}\frac{L^{2}}{u^{1-\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2^{\gamma(j_{n,\delta}+1)}\gamma}\lesssim_{\gamma}L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma},

\displaystyle\int_{a_{j_{n,\delta}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{u_{j_{n,\delta}}}^{1}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}\leq\int_{1-2^{-(j_{n,\delta}+1)}}^{1}\frac{L^{2}}{(1-u)^{1-\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}u\lesssim_{\gamma}L^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma}.

(47)

Moreover, (
7
) and Proposition
12
(c)
ensure that on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
of probability at least
1-\delta
defined in (
17
), the multiscale estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}
satisfies

\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}-\psi_{0})^{2}f_{0}\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}=\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}i(f_{0})\leq r\wedge\frac{2^{1-\gamma}L^{2}}{\gamma}=\tilde{r},

so
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})\lesssim_{\gamma}\bar{r}
. In combination with (
45
)–(
47
), we conclude from Proposition
12
(b,c)
that on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, the estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)

\displaystyle-\psi_{0}(x)\bigr)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl\{\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{-j_{n,\delta}}}^{a_{j_{n,\delta}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{-\infty}^{a_{-j_{n,\delta}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}+\int_{a_{j_{n,\delta}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\biggr\}\wedge\tilde{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\biggl\{\tilde{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/3}}{\tilde{r}^{1/3}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/3}+\tilde{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\Bigr)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}+M+\tilde{r}\,\frac{L^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\gamma}\biggr\}\wedge\tilde{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl\{\tilde{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\tilde{r}^{1/\gamma}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}}+M\biggr\}\wedge\tilde{r}\asymp_{\gamma}M\wedge\bar{r}.

(48)

This yields the upper bound (
8
) on the minimax
(1-\delta)
th quantile
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})
for every
\gamma\in(0,1]
. In particular, when
r=2^{1-\gamma}L^{2}/\gamma
, we have
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}=\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L}
, so (
8
) simplifies to the bound (
9
) on
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L})
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4
.

We use the notation and definitions in the statement and proof of Theorem
3
. For
B\in(0,\infty]
, consider the projected estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}:=(-B)\vee\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}\wedge B
. On the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, Proposition
12
(c)
ensures that

\bigl|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}(x)\bigr|\leq|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)|\leq|\psi_{0}(x)|

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
; moreover, if
x\in[a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}},a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}]
, then by (
6
),

\displaystyle\bigl|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)\bigr|\leq\bigl|\psi_{0}(x)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq|\psi_{0}(a_{-j_{n,\delta}})|\vee|\psi_{0}(a_{j_{n,\delta}})|=|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{-j_{n,\delta}})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j_{n,\delta}})|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{(j_{n,\delta}+1)(1-\gamma)}{2}}L\leq\min\bigl(2^{11/2}C_{0}^{3/2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta},1\bigr)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}L=:B_{n,\delta}.

Thus, if
B\geq B_{n,\delta}
, then
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}(x)=\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)
for all
x\in[a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}},a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}]
, so by (
48
) and (
47
),

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}-\psi_{0}\bigr)^{2}f_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\int_{a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}}}^{a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}}(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}-\psi_{0})^{2}f_{0}+\int_{-\infty}^{a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}+\int_{a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\biggr\}\lesssim_{\gamma}B\wedge\bar{r}

(49)

on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
. In other words, the upper bound on
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})
is achieved by
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}
for any
B\geq B_{n,\delta}
.

Next, by considering the trivial estimator
\tilde{\psi}\equiv 0
, we deduce from (
7
) that

\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\leq r\wedge\frac{2^{1-\gamma}L^{2}}{\gamma}=\tilde{r}.

Next, taking
\delta=1/n
and
B:=B_{n,\delta}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}^{c})\leq\delta=1/n
by Proposition
11
,
and

\varepsilon_{n,\delta}=\frac{2\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{9n}=\frac{2\log n}{3n},\qquad|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}|\leq B\asymp L(\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\wedge 1)^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\asymp L\Bigl(\frac{n}{\log n}\Bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}.

Thus, by (
49
) and (
7
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}-\psi_{0}\bigr)^{2}f_{0}\biggr)\wedge\tilde{r}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}^{c}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(B-\psi_{0})^{2}f_{0}\biggr)+\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}-\psi_{0}\bigr)^{2}f_{0}\biggr)\biggr\}\wedge\tilde{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\biggl\{\delta\Bigl(B^{2}+\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\Bigr)+\bar{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}}\log_{+}^{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\gamma=1/3\}}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr),\,\bar{r}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\bar{r}\min\biggl\{\frac{L^{2}}{\bar{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Bigl(\frac{n}{\log n}\Bigr)^{1-\gamma}+\frac{1}{n}+\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{\log n}{n}\Bigr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}}\log^{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\gamma=1/3\}}}\Bigl(\frac{n}{\log n}\Bigr),\,1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma}\bar{r}\min\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}}\log^{c_{\gamma}}n,\,1\Bigr\},

as required.
∎

A.1.2
Proofs for Section
2.3

The proof strategy for Theorem
7
is analogous to that for Theorem
3
, in that we employ the same sequence
(a_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
as in Lemma
15
but use Lemma
26
instead of (
6
) to bound terms involving
J_{0}^{\prime}
and
J_{0}
. The Hölder condition restricts the range of indices
j
for which Proposition
13
is applicable on
[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
via Lemma
17
; see (
51
) below.

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Consider an arbitrary
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}
. We first assemble some useful bounds that will be invoked repeatedly later on. First, since
C_{\beta}\leq 2/\sqrt{\pi}
and
\log_{+}(\lambda x)\leq\lambda\log_{+}(x)
for
\lambda\geq 1
and
x>0
,
it follows from the definition of
u_{j}
for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and (
108
) and (
109
) in Lemma
26
that

\begin{split}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j})|&\leq 2L^{1/\beta}\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}(2^{|j|+1}C_{\beta})\leq 3L^{1/\beta}(|j|\vee 1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta},\\
J_{0}(u_{j})&\leq 2^{-|j|+1}L^{1/\beta}\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}(2^{|j|+1}C_{\beta})\leq 2^{-|j|}L^{1/\beta}(|j|\vee 1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}.\end{split}

(50)

Fix
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
and let
j_{n,\delta}:=\big\lfloor\log_{2}(2^{-13/2}C_{0}^{-3/2}/\varepsilon_{n,\delta})\big\rfloor_{+}
. If
j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j_{n,\delta}-1\}
, then for every
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
, we have
C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}/f_{0}(x)\leq\kappa_{j}
by Lemma
17
(c)
, so the infimum in (
36
) is taken over a non-empty set of bandwidths
h
. Since
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}
, the function
g_{j}\colon x\mapsto\psi_{0}(x)f_{0}(a_{j})^{1/2}
in Lemma
17
satisfies
|g_{j}(x)-g_{j}(y)|\leq Lf_{0}(a_{j})^{1/2}|x-y|^{\beta-1}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, so we seek to apply Proposition
13
to
g\equiv g_{j}
on
[a_{j}-\kappa_{j},a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j}]
with
L_{*}\equiv Lf_{0}(a_{j})^{1/2}
and
V\equiv V_{j}=g_{j}(a_{j}-\kappa_{j})-g_{j}(a_{j+1}+\kappa_{j})
.
To this end, observe that whenever

J_{0}(u_{j})\geq L^{1/\beta}(2C_{0})^{(2\beta+1)/(2\beta)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta},

(51)

it follows from (
27
) in Lemma
15
that every
x\in[a_{j},a_{j+1}]
satisfies

\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{2C_{0}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{f_{0}(a_{j})}\leq\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{L^{2}f_{0}(a_{j})}\Bigr)^{1/(2\beta+1)}.

(52)

If there exists
j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j_{n,\delta}-1\}
for which (
51
) holds, then let
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
be the minimum and maximum such indices respectively. Otherwise, let
j^{\prime}=j^{\prime\prime}:=1
.
Then by (
50
),
j_{*}:=\max(|j^{\prime}|,|j^{\prime\prime}|)
satisfies
(|j_{*}|\vee 1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}2^{-|j_{*}|}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

and hence

2^{|j_{*}|}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\log_{+}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr).

(53)

If
j^{\prime}\leq j<j^{\prime\prime}
,
then by Lemma
17
(a)
, (
52
), Proposition
13
, Lemma
17
(b)
and (
29
) in Lemma
15
,

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38092pt\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\inf\Bigl\{\bigl(g_{j}(x)-g_{j}(x-\omega h)\bigr)^{2}\vee\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{h^{3}}:\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{L^{2}f_{0}(a_{j})}\Bigr)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\wedge\kappa_{j}\leq h\leq\kappa_{j}\Bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim V_{j}L^{1/(2\beta+1)}f_{0}(a_{j})^{1/(4\beta+2)}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}+\frac{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}{\kappa_{j}^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{1/(2\beta+1)}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)\bigl(J_{0}(u_{j})^{\beta+1}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta}\bigr)^{1/(2\beta+1)}+2^{2(|j|+3)}J_{0}(u_{j})^{2}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}.

(54)

Similarly to Theorem
3
, we will bound (
54
) in two different ways depending on the value of
j
. First, recalling from (
110
) that
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}
satisfies
i(f_{0})\leq r\wedge 8L^{2/\beta}=:s
, let
j_{L}:=\min\bigl\{j\in\mathbb{N}:2^{j}\geq(L^{2/\beta}/s)\log_{+}^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}(L^{2/\beta}/s)\bigr\}
.
If
j\in\{0,\dotsc,j_{L}\}
, then by (
32
), (
50
), the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
33
),

\displaystyle 2^{j/2}J_{0}(u_{j})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j/2}J_{0}(u_{j_{L}})+2^{j/2}\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{L}}2^{-\ell}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{1/\beta}2^{-j_{L}/2}j_{L}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}+\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{L}}2^{-(\ell-j)/2}\cdot 2^{-\ell/2}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{1/2}+\biggl(\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{L}}2^{-(\ell-j)}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sum_{\ell=j}^{j_{L}}2^{-\ell}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{\ell})^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\lesssim s^{1/2}+i(f_{0})^{1/2}\lesssim s^{1/2}.

(55)

By another application of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
33
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{j_{L}}2^{-\frac{j(\beta+1)}{2(2\beta+1)}}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)

\displaystyle=2^{\frac{j_{L}\beta}{2(2\beta+1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{j_{L}}2^{-\frac{(j_{L}-j)\beta}{2(2\beta+1)}}\cdot 2^{-j/2}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{j_{L}\beta}{2(2\beta+1)}}\biggl(\sum_{j=0}^{j_{L}}2^{-\frac{(j_{L}-j)\beta}{2\beta+1}}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sum_{j=-1}^{j_{L}+2}2^{-j}J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j})^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{L}{s^{\beta/2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)s^{1/2}.

(56)

The same bounds hold for
j\in\{-j_{L},\dotsc,-1\}
by analogous reasoning, so we deduce from (
54
)–(
56
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=j^{\prime}\vee-j_{L}}^{(j^{\prime\prime}\wedge j_{L})-1}

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim(L\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}\sum_{j=-j_{L}}^{j_{L}}J_{0}(u_{j})^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)+s\sum_{j=-j_{L}}^{j_{L}}2^{|j|}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim(L\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta}s^{\frac{\beta+1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}\sum_{j=-j_{L}}^{j_{L}}2^{-\frac{|j|(\beta+1)}{2(2\beta+1)}}\bigl(|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j-1})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j+2})|\bigr)+L^{2/\beta}\log_{+}^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)+s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)\log_{+}^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr).

(57)

Alternatively, for
j^{\prime}\leq j<j^{\prime\prime}
, we can apply (
50
) directly to (
54
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}

\displaystyle\int_{a_{j}}^{a_{j+1}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}L^{1/\beta}(|j|\vee 1)^{1-\frac{1}{\beta}}\bigl(2^{-|j|}(|j|\vee 1)^{1-\frac{1}{\beta}}L^{1/\beta}\bigr)^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}+L^{2/\beta}(|j|\vee 1)^{2(1-\frac{1}{\beta})}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle=L^{2/\beta}\bigl\{2^{-|j|\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}(|j|\vee 1)^{\frac{(3\beta+2)(\beta-1)}{\beta(2\beta+1)}}\,\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}+(|j|\vee 1)^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}.

(58)

If
(j_{1},j_{2})=(j^{\prime},j^{\prime}\vee-j_{L})
or
(j_{1},j_{2})=(j^{\prime\prime}\wedge j_{L},j^{\prime\prime})
and
j_{1}<j_{2}
, then
j_{*}\geq 1
, and by (
53
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=j_{1}}^{j_{2}-1}L^{2/\beta}

\displaystyle\bigl\{2^{-|j|\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}(|j|\vee 1)^{\frac{(3\beta+2)(\beta-1)}{\beta(2\beta+1)}}\,\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}+(|j|\vee 1)^{2(\beta-1)\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}L^{2/\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}2^{-j_{L}\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}j_{L}^{\frac{(3\beta+2)(\beta-1)}{\beta(2\beta+1)}}+L^{2/\beta}j_{*}^{(3\beta-2)/\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)+s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)\log_{+}^{(3\beta-2)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr).

Together with (
57
) and (
58
), this yields

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j^{\prime}}}^{a_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)\Bigl\{\log_{+}^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)+\log_{+}^{(3\beta-2)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

(59)

Next, recall that
j^{\prime}\geq-j_{n,\delta}
by definition. If
j^{\prime}>-j_{n,\delta}
, then in both of the cases
j^{\prime}<j^{\prime\prime}
and
j^{\prime}=j^{\prime\prime}
, (
51
) does not hold for any
j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j^{\prime}-1\}
, so by (
27
) in Lemma
15
,

\sup_{u\in(u_{-j_{n,\delta}},u_{j^{\prime}}]}J_{0}(u)\leq 2\max_{j\in\{-j_{n,\delta},\dotsc,j^{\prime}-1\}}J_{0}(u_{j})\lesssim L^{1/\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}.

Therefore, by Lemma
14
(b)
, (
108
), a calculation similar to (
112
) in the proof of Lemma
26
, and finally (
53
), it follows in both cases that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{a_{j^{\prime}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}=\int_{0}^{u_{j^{\prime}}}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u_{-j_{n,\delta}}}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}+\sup_{u\in(u_{-j_{n,\delta}},u_{j^{\prime}}]}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|\int_{u_{-j_{n,\delta}}}^{u_{j^{\prime}}}|J_{0}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2/\beta}\int_{0}^{u_{-j_{n,\delta}}}\log_{+}^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}u+L^{1/\beta}j_{n,\delta}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\sup_{u\in(u_{-j_{n,\delta}},u_{j^{\prime}}]}J_{0}(u)

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2/\beta}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\log_{+}^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr).

(60)

By analogous reasoning, the same bound holds for
\int_{a_{j^{\prime\prime}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}
. Moreover, by Proposition
12
(c)
and (
110
) in Lemma
26
,

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)-\psi_{0}(x)\bigr)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq i(f_{0})\leq r\wedge 8L^{2/\beta}=s.

Combining this with (
59
) and (
60
), we deduce from Proposition
12
(b,c)
that on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
of probability at least
1-\delta
, the estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84544pt\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)-\psi_{0}(x)\bigr)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl\{\sum_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{a_{j^{\prime}}}^{a_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x)^{2}f_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{-\infty}^{a_{j^{\prime}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}+\int_{a_{j^{\prime\prime}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\biggr\}\wedge s

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\biggl\{s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)+s\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)\biggl(\log_{+}^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{\beta}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\Bigr)+\log_{+}^{3-\frac{2}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr)}\biggr)\biggr\}\wedge s

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\Bigr)+\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\log_{+}^{3-\frac{2}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr),\,1\biggr\}.

(61)

This yields the required upper bound (
11
) on the minimax
(1-\delta)
th quantile
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})
. In particular, when
r=8L^{2/\beta}
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}=\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}
, so (
61
) simplifies to a bound of order
L^{2/\beta}(\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\wedge 1)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}
on
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L})
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
8
.

We argue similarly to the proof of Corollary
4
. Proposition
12
(c)
ensures that on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, the projected estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}:=(-B)\vee\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}\wedge B
with
B\in(0,\infty]
satisfies

\bigl|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}(x)\bigr|\leq|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)|\leq|\psi_{0}(x)|

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, if
x\in[a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}},a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}]
, then by (
50
),

\displaystyle\bigl|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)\bigr|\leq\bigl|\psi_{0}(x)\bigr|\leq|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{-j_{n,\delta}})|\vee|J_{0}^{\prime}(u_{j_{n,\delta}})|\leq 3L^{1/\beta}(j_{n,\delta}\vee 1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}=:B_{n,\delta}^{\prime}

on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
. Thus, if
B\geq B_{n,\delta}^{\prime}
, then
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}(x)=\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x)
for all
x\in[a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}},a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}]
, so by (
61
) and (
60
),

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}-\psi_{0}\bigr)^{2}f_{0}\leq\biggl\{\int_{a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}}}^{a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}}(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}-\psi_{0})^{2}f_{0}+\int_{-\infty}^{a_{-{j_{n,\delta}}}}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}+\int_{a_{{j_{n,\delta}}}}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}f_{0}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\lesssim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\Bigr)+\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\log_{+}^{3-\frac{2}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr),\,1\biggr\}

(62)

on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
. In other words, the upper bound on
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})
is achieved by
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}
for any
B\geq B_{n,\delta}^{\prime}
.
Moreover, by considering the estimator
\tilde{\psi}\equiv 0
, we deduce from (
110
) that
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}}i(f_{0})\leq s
. Now let
\delta=1/n

and
B=B_{n,\delta}^{\prime}\asymp L^{1/\beta}\log_{+}^{1-\frac{1}{\beta}}(1/\varepsilon_{n,\delta})\asymp L^{1/\beta}\log^{1-\frac{1}{\beta}}n
.
Then by Proposition
11
and the fact that
|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}|\leq B
, together with (
110
) and (
62
),

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(B)}-\psi_{0}\bigr)^{2}f_{0}\biggr)\wedge s

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\min\biggl\{(B^{2}+r^{\prime})\,\delta+r^{\prime}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\Bigr)+r^{\prime}\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{n,\delta}\log_{+}^{3-\frac{2}{\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n,\delta}}\Bigr),\,r^{\prime}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log^{2(1-\frac{1}{\beta})}n}{n}+\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log n}{n}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\log_{+}^{\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+1}}n+\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log n}{n}\log^{3-\frac{2}{\beta}}n,\,1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log^{(4\beta-2)/\beta}n}{n}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)},\,1\biggr\},

as required.
∎

A.2
Proofs of minimax lower bounds

A.2.1
Proofs for Sections
2.1
and
2.2

We begin with a lemma based on the construction discussed in Section
2.1
, which is used to prove the lower bounds over both the class
\mathcal{F}
in Proposition
1
and
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}
in Theorem
5
.

Lemma 18
.

Given
a\geq\sqrt{2}
and
h\in[0,1/a]
, define
x_{a}:=a-\frac{1}{a}
and
\psi_{a,h},\phi_{a,h}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\psi_{a,h}(x):=-\sgn(x)\Bigl(a\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\geq x_{a}+h\}}+\frac{a}{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\in(x_{a}-h,x_{a}+h)\}}\Bigr),\qquad\phi_{a,h}(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{a,h}.

Then
f_{a,h}:=e^{\phi_{a,h}}/\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{a,h}}
is a symmetric density with antisymmetric score function
\psi_{a,h}
, and
f_{a,h}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2}
for any
\gamma\in(0,1]
. Moreover, the corresponding distribution
P_{a,h}
satisfies

\chi^{2}(P_{a,h},P_{a,0}):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{f_{a,h}}{f_{a,0}}-1\Bigr)^{2}f_{a,0}<2ah^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{a,0}-\psi_{a,h})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h})\geq\frac{ah}{2e}.

(63)

Consequently, for
\delta\in(0,1/4)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in(0,1/a]}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\{f_{a,0},f_{a,h}\})\gtrsim\Bigl(\frac{a^{2}}{n}\Bigr)^{1/3}\wedge 1,\quad\;\;\sup_{h\in(0,1/a]}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\{f_{a,0},f_{a,h}\})\gtrsim\Bigl(\frac{a^{2}\log(1/\delta)}{n}\Bigr)^{1/3}\wedge 1.

The functions
\phi_{a,h}
and
\psi_{a,h}
are illustrated in Figure
1
in the main text.

Proof.

Since
h\in[0,1/a]
, we have
x_{a}-h\geq a-\frac{2}{a}\geq 0
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\phi_{a,0}(x)=-a(|x|-x_{a})_{+}
and

\phi_{a,h}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{a,h}=\phi_{a,0}(x)-\frac{a}{2}\bigl(h-\bigl||x|-x_{a}\bigr|\bigr)_{+}\leq\phi_{a,0}(x),

with equality if and only if
|x|\notin(x_{a}-h,x_{a}+h)
. Therefore,

C_{h}:=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{a,h}}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{a,0}}=2x_{a}+2\int_{x_{a}}^{\infty}e^{-a(x-x_{a})}=2a=:C_{0},

so because
\psi_{a,h}
is decreasing,
f_{a,h}
is a continuous and symmetric log-concave density with antisymmetric score function
\psi_{a,h}
. Since
e^{\phi_{a,h}}\leq e^{\phi_{a,0}}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle C_{0}-C_{h}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(e^{\phi_{a,0}}-e^{\phi_{a,h}})\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\phi_{a,0}-\phi_{a,h})=a\int_{-h}^{h}(h-|x|)\,\mathrm{d}x=ah^{2}\leq\frac{1}{a}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{4}.

(64)

For
\gamma\in(0,1)
, we claim that
f_{a,h}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2}
for all
h\in[0,1/a]
. Indeed, since
f_{a,h}
is symmetric, the corresponding distribution function
F_{a,h}
satisfies
F_{a,h}(x)=1-F_{a,h}(-x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
. First consider
h=0
. If
x\geq x_{a}
, then
F_{a,0}(-x)\leq 1/2\leq F_{a,0}(x)
and

\displaystyle F_{a,0}(-x)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}=\bigl(1-F_{a,0}(x)\bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{1}{2a}\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-a(z-x_{a})}\,\mathrm{d}z\biggr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}=\frac{2^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}a^{\gamma}}{a}=\frac{2^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}a^{\gamma}}{|\psi_{a,0}(x)|}\leq\frac{2^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}a^{\gamma}}{|\psi_{a,0}(-x)|}.

Moreover, if
x\in[0,x_{a})
, then
|\psi_{a,0}(x)|=0\leq a^{\gamma}/\min\bigl(F_{a,0}(x),1-F_{a,0}(x)\bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}
. Since
i(f_{a,0})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{a,0}^{2}f_{a,0}=2a^{2}\int_{x_{a}}^{\infty}f_{a,0}=1
, we have
f_{a,0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},1}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2}
.

Now consider
h\in(0,1/a]
. If
x\leq 0
, then
F_{a,h}(x)\leq 1/2\leq F_{a,h}(-x)
, and by (
64
),

\displaystyle 1-F_{a,h}(-x)=F_{a,h}(x)=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\phi_{a,h}}}{C_{h}}

\displaystyle<\frac{2\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\phi_{a,0}}}{C_{0}}

\displaystyle=2F_{a,0}(x)\leq\Bigl(\frac{a^{\gamma}}{|\psi_{a,h}(x)|}\Bigr)^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}=\Bigl(\frac{a^{\gamma}}{|\psi_{a,h}(-x)|}\Bigr)^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}.

For
x\in[-x_{a}+h,0]
, we have
\frac{1}{2}-F_{a,h}(x)=\int_{x}^{0}f_{a,h}=\int_{x}^{0}e^{\phi_{a,0}}/C_{h}\geq\int_{x}^{0}e^{\phi_{a,0}}/C_{0}=\frac{1}{2}-F_{a,0}(x)
, so because
|\psi_{a,h}(x)|\leq a\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\geq x_{a}-h\}}
, we have

\displaystyle i(f_{a,h})=2a^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{-x_{a}+h}f_{a,h}

\displaystyle=2a^{2}F_{a,h}(-x_{a}+h)\leq 2a^{2}F_{a,0}(-x_{a}+h)

\displaystyle=2a^{2}\Bigl(\int_{-\infty}^{-x_{a}}\frac{e^{a(x_{a}+x)}}{2a}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{-x_{a}}^{-x_{a}+h}\frac{1}{2a}\Bigr)=1+ah\leq 2.

Thus,
f_{a,h}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2}
, as claimed.

Next, by (
64
),
c_{h}:=C_{0}/C_{h}\in[1,4/3]
and
\log f_{a,h}-\log f_{a,0}=\phi_{a,h}-\phi_{a,0}+\log c_{h}\leq\log(4/3)
. Moreover,

c_{h}-1\leq\frac{4(C_{0}-C_{h})}{3C_{0}}\leq\frac{4ah^{2}}{3C_{0}}=\frac{2h^{2}}{3}.

Since
f_{a,0}\leq 1/C_{0}=1/(2a)
on
\mathbb{R}
and
|e^{x}-1|\leq 4|x|/3
for
x\in(-\infty,\log(4/3)]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(P_{a,h},P_{a,0})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{f_{a,h}}{f_{a,0}}-1\Bigr)^{2}f_{a,0}\leq 2\int_{x_{a}-h}^{x_{a}+h}(e^{\phi_{a,h}-\phi_{a,0}+\log c_{h}}-1)^{2}\,\frac{1}{2a}+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(c_{h}-1)^{2}f_{a,0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{9a}\int_{x_{a}-h}^{x_{a}+h}(\phi_{a,h}-\phi_{a,0}+\log c_{h})^{2}+(c_{h}-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{9a}\biggl(\frac{a^{2}}{2}\int_{-h}^{h}(h-|x|)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+4h\log^{2}c_{h}\biggr)+\frac{4h^{4}}{9}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{9a}\Bigl(\frac{a^{2}h^{3}}{3}+\frac{16h^{5}}{9}\Bigr)+\frac{4h^{4}}{9}=ah^{3}\Bigl(\frac{16}{27}+\frac{256h^{2}}{81a^{2}}+\frac{4h}{9a}\Bigr)<2ah^{3}.

Moreover,
|\psi_{a,0}(x)-\psi_{a,h}(x)|=a\mathbbm{1}_{\|x|\in(x_{a}-h,x_{a}+h\}}/2
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. If
|x|\in[x_{a}-h,x_{a}+h]
, then

f_{a,h}(x)=\frac{e^{\phi_{a,h}(x)}}{2a}\geq\frac{e^{\phi_{a,0}(x_{a}+h)}}{2a}=\frac{e^{-ah}}{2a}\geq\frac{1}{2ea}

for all
h\in[0,1/a]
. Therefore,

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{a,0}-\psi_{a,h})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h})\geq\frac{a^{2}}{2}\int_{x_{a}-h}^{x_{a}+h}f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h}\geq\frac{ah}{2e},

which establishes (
63
). Finally, taking

h^{\prime}:=\Bigl(\frac{1}{2an}\Bigr)^{1/3}\wedge\frac{1}{a},

we have
h^{\prime}\in(0,1/a]
and
\KL(P_{a,h^{\prime}}^{\otimes n},P_{a,0}^{\otimes n})=n\KL(P_{a,h^{\prime}},P_{a,0})\leq n\chi^{2}(P_{a,h^{\prime}},P_{a,0})<1
, so by Le Cam’s two-point lemma
(e.g.
61
, Lemma 8.4)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\{f_{a,0},f_{a,h^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{h\in\{0,h^{\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}_{f_{a,h}}\biggl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{a,h})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h^{\prime}})\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{a,0}-\psi_{a,h^{\prime}})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h^{\prime}})\geq\frac{ah^{\prime}}{32e}=\frac{1}{32e}\min\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{a^{2}}{2n}\Bigr)^{1/3},\,1\Bigr\}.

On the other hand, given
\delta\in(0,1/4]
, let

h^{\prime\prime}:=\Bigl\{\frac{1}{2an}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}^{1/3}\wedge\frac{1}{a}.

Then
\KL(P_{a,h^{\prime\prime}}^{\otimes n},P_{a,0}^{\otimes n})\leq n\chi^{2}(P_{a,h^{\prime\prime}},P_{a,0})<\log\bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\bigr)
, so by
53
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\{f_{a,0},f_{a,h^{\prime\prime}}\})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{h\in\{0,h^{\prime\prime}\}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{a,h}}\biggl(1-\delta,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{a,h})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h^{\prime\prime}})\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{a,0}-\psi_{a,h^{\prime\prime}})^{2}(f_{a,0}\wedge f_{a,h^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{ah^{\prime\prime}}{8e}=\frac{1}{8e}\min\biggl(\Bigl\{\frac{a^{2}}{2n}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}^{1/3},\,1\biggr).

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1
.

By Lemma
18
with
a=(2\vee n)^{1/2}
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\wedge\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{2})\gtrsim 1
. For general
r\in(0,\infty)
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{r}=\{(r/2)^{1/2}f_{0}\bigl((r/2)^{1/2}\cdot\bigr):f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\}
, so

\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{r})=\frac{r}{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\gtrsim r,\qquad\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{r})=\frac{r}{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{2})\gtrsim r.

On the other hand, by considering the estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n}\equiv 0
, we obtain
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{r})\vee\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{r})\leq r
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5
.

For both the minimax risk and minimax quantile, we obtain two separate lower bounds and take their maximum at the end of the proof to establish the desired conclusion. The first bound follows directly from Lemma
18
, while the second is derived by applying Le Cam’s two-point lemma and its high-probability analogue
(
53
, Theorem 4 and Corollary 6)
to a perturbation of a different base density defined below.

Figure 3:
The log-densities
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}
(left) and corresponding score functions
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
(right) used in the proof of Theorem
5
.

Given
\tilde{L}\geq 1
, let
b:=(1+\gamma)/(1-\gamma)>1
and define
g_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

g_{0}(x):=\begin{cases}\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{+}^{b}\;&\text{if }x\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}\\
\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}\;&\text{if }x\in(\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma},x^{\prime}],\\
g_{0}(2x^{\prime}-x)\;&\text{if }x>x^{\prime},\end{cases}

where
x^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{L}^{1/\gamma}+(1-\frac{1-\gamma}{2})\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}\geq\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}
. Then
g_{0}
is a continuous density on
\mathbb{R}
whose score function
\psi_{0}
satisfies

\psi_{0}(x)=\frac{g_{0}^{\prime}(x)}{g_{0}(x)}=\begin{cases}\,\dfrac{b}{x}\;&\text{if }x\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma})\\[6.0pt]
\,\,0\;&\text{if }x\in(\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma},x^{\prime}]\\[3.0pt]
-\psi_{0}(2x^{\prime}-x)\;&\text{if }x\in(x^{\prime},2x^{\prime})\setminus\{2x^{\prime}-\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}\};\end{cases}

see Figure
3
.
Thus,
\psi_{0}
is decreasing on
(0,2x^{\prime})
, so
g_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
. Next, for some
x_{0}\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}/2]
that we will specify later on, let
\phi_{0}:=\log g_{0}
and define
\phi_{1}\colon(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
by

\phi_{1}(x):=\begin{cases}\dfrac{2x_{0}-x}{x_{0}}\phi_{0}(x_{0})+\dfrac{x-x_{0}}{x_{0}}\phi_{0}(2x_{0})=\phi_{0}(x_{0})+\dfrac{x-x_{0}}{x_{0}}\,b\log 2\;&\text{if }x\in[x_{0},2x_{0}]\\[5.0pt]
\,\phi_{0}(x)\;&\text{if }x\in(0,x^{\prime}]\setminus(x_{0},2x_{0})\\[3.0pt]
\phi_{1}(2x^{\prime}-x)\;&\text{if }x>x^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Since
\phi_{0}
is concave and continuous on
(0,2x^{\prime})
, the same is true of
\phi_{1}
, and
\phi_{1}\leq\phi_{0}
on
(0,2x^{\prime})
. Thus,
C_{1}:=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{1}}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{0}}=1
, and
g_{1}:=e^{\phi_{1}}/C_{1}\in\mathcal{F}
has an antisymmetric score function
\psi_{1}
satisfying

\psi_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}\dfrac{b\log 2}{x_{0}}\;&\text{if }x\in(x_{0},2x_{0})\\[6.0pt]
\,\psi_{0}(x)\;&\text{if }x\in(0,x^{\prime}]\setminus[x_{0},2x_{0}]\\[3.0pt]
-\psi_{1}(2x^{\prime}-x)\;&\text{if }x\in(x^{\prime},2x^{\prime}).\end{cases}

It follows that

\displaystyle 1-C_{1}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(e^{\phi_{0}}-e^{\phi_{1}})

\displaystyle=2\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}\biggl(\tilde{L}^{b+1}x^{b}-\exp\Bigl\{\log(\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{0}^{b})+\frac{x-x_{0}}{x_{0}}\,b\log 2\Bigr\}\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{0}^{b+1}\int_{1}^{2}(y^{b}-2^{b(y-1)})\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{c_{b}}{2}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{2/(1-\gamma)},

where
c_{b}:=1\vee\bigl(4\int_{1}^{2}(y^{b}-2^{b(y-1)})\,\mathrm{d}y\bigr)>0
since
y>2^{y-1}
for
y\in(1,2)
. Therefore, if
\tilde{L}\geq c_{b}^{\gamma/2}
, then
C_{1}=1-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{2/(1-\gamma)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\tilde{L}^{-2/\gamma}\geq 1/2
.

Now given any
L\geq 2c_{b}^{\gamma/2}(1-\gamma)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}b\log 2=:L_{\gamma}
, define

\tilde{L}:=\frac{L}{2(1-\gamma)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}b\log 2}\geq c_{b}^{\gamma/2},\qquad r_{\gamma}:=\frac{(4b\log 2)^{2}}{b-1}.

We claim that
g_{0},g_{1}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r_{\gamma}}
. Indeed,
g_{0},g_{1}
are symmetric about
x^{\prime}
by construction, so the corresponding distribution functions
G_{0},G_{1}
satisfy
G_{k}(x)=1-G_{k}(2x^{\prime}-x)\leq 1/2
for
x\in[0,x^{\prime}]
and
k\in\{0,1\}
. If
x\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma})
, then

\displaystyle G_{0}(x)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}=\biggl(\int_{0}^{x}\tilde{L}^{b+1}z^{b}\,\mathrm{d}z\biggr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}=\tilde{L}x\Bigl(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}=\frac{\tilde{L}b}{|\psi_{0}(x)|}\Bigl(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}.

For such
x
, we also have
0<\psi_{1}(x)\leq(2\log 2)\psi_{0}(x)
, so

G_{1}(x)=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\phi_{1}}}{C_{1}}\leq\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\phi_{0}}}{C_{1}}\leq 2G_{0}(x)=(1-\gamma)\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}b}{|\psi_{0}(x)|}\Bigr)^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}\leq\Bigl(\frac{L}{|\psi_{1}(x)|}\Bigr)^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}.

Moreover,
\psi_{0}(x)=\psi_{1}(x)=0
for
x\in(\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma},x^{\prime}]
. On the other hand, if
x\in(x^{\prime},2x^{\prime})
and
k\in\{0,1\}
, then
G_{k}(x)=1-G_{k}(2x^{\prime}-x)\geq 1/2
and

\displaystyle|\psi_{k}(x)|

\displaystyle=|\psi_{k}(2x^{\prime}-x)|\leq\frac{L}{G_{k}(2x^{\prime}-x)^{(1-\gamma)/2}}=\frac{L}{\bigl(1-G_{k}(x)\bigr)^{(1-\gamma)/2}}.

Therefore, for any
u\in(0,1)
and
k\in\{0,1\}
, we deduce by taking
x=G_{k}^{-1}(u)\in(0,2x^{\prime})
that

|J_{k}^{\prime}(u)|=|\psi_{k}(x)|\leq\frac{L}{G_{k}(x)^{(1-\gamma)/2}\wedge\bigl(1-G_{k}(x)\bigr)^{(1-\gamma)/2}}=\frac{L}{u^{(1-\gamma)/2}\wedge(1-u)^{(1-\gamma)/2}}.

Since

g_{1}=e^{\phi_{1}}/C_{1}\leq 2e^{\phi_{0}}=2g_{0}
on
(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma})
and since
\psi_{1}=\psi_{0}=0
on
(\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma},x^{\prime}]
, we also have

\displaystyle i(g_{1})=2\int_{0}^{\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}}\psi_{1}^{2}\,g_{1}

\displaystyle\leq(4\log 2)^{2}\int_{0}^{\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}}\psi_{0}^{2}\,g_{0}=(8\log^{2}2)\,i(g_{0})

\displaystyle=(4\log 2)^{2}\int_{0}^{\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}}\tilde{L}^{b+1}b^{2}x^{b-2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{(4b\log 2)^{2}}{b-1}.

Thus,
g_{0},g_{1}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r_{\gamma}}
, as required.

Next,
\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\log C_{1}\leq\log 2
on
(0,2x^{\prime})
and

\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}(\phi_{0}-\phi_{1})^{2}=\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}\biggl(b\log\frac{x}{x_{0}}-\Bigl(\frac{x}{x_{0}}-1\Bigr)b\log 2\biggr)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\frac{b^{2}x_{0}}{527}.

Hence, denoting by
Q_{k}
the probability measure with density
g_{k}
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(Q_{1},Q_{0})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{g_{1}}{g_{0}}-1\Bigr)^{2}g_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}(e^{\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\log C_{1}}-1)^{2}\,\tilde{L}^{b+1}x^{b}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{1}{C_{1}}-1\Bigr)^{2}g_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 8\tilde{L}^{b+1}(2x_{0})^{b}\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\log C_{1})^{2}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{C_{1}}-1\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 16\tilde{L}^{b+1}(2x_{0})^{b}\biggl(x_{0}\log^{2}C_{1}+\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})^{2}\biggr)+4(1-C_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle<16\tilde{L}^{b+1}(2x_{0})^{b}\Bigl(x_{0}(4\log^{2}2)(1-C_{1})^{2}+\frac{b^{2}x_{0}}{527}\Bigr)+c_{b}^{2}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{\frac{4}{1-\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{b+4}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}\Bigl(\log^{2}2+\frac{b^{2}}{527}+\frac{c_{b}^{2}}{2^{b+4}}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}\Bigr)\leq c_{b}^{\prime}(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}},

where we can take
c_{b}^{\prime}:=2^{b+4}(\log^{2}2+b^{2}/527)+c_{b}
since
(\tilde{L}x_{0})^{2/(1-\gamma)}\leq\tilde{L}^{-2/\gamma}\leq 1/c_{b}
.

Thus, letting

x_{0}:=\frac{1}{\tilde{L}}\Bigl\{\frac{1}{nc_{b}^{\prime}}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}^{(1-\gamma)/2}\wedge\frac{\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}}{2},

we have
2x_{0}\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}
, and

\displaystyle\KL(Q_{1}^{\otimes n},Q_{0}^{\otimes n})=n\KL(Q_{1},Q_{0})\leq n\chi^{2}(Q_{1},Q_{0})<\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr).

Since
g_{0}(x)\wedge g_{1}(x)\geq g_{0}(x_{0})\geq\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{0}^{b}
for
x\in[x_{0},2x_{0}]
, it follows from
53
that for any
\delta\in(0,1/4]
and
L\geq L_{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r_{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{j\in\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{g_{j}}\biggl(1-\delta,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{j})^{2}(g_{0}\wedge g_{1})\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{0}-\psi_{1})^{2}(g_{0}\wedge g_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{x_{0}}^{2x_{0}}\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{0}^{b}\,\Bigl(\frac{b}{x}-\frac{b\log 2}{x_{0}}\Bigr)^{2}\,dx=\frac{b^{2}(1-2\log^{2}2)}{4}\,\tilde{L}^{b+1}x_{0}^{b-1}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\min\biggl\{L^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\Bigr)^{\gamma},\,1\biggr\}.

On the other hand, taking

x_{0}:=\frac{1}{\tilde{L}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{nc_{b}^{\prime}}\Bigr)^{(1-\gamma)/2}\wedge\frac{\tilde{L}^{-1/\gamma}}{2},

we have
\KL(Q_{1}^{\otimes n},Q_{0}^{\otimes n})\leq 1
, so by Le Cam’s two-point lemma
(e.g.
61
, Lemma 8.4)
and a similar calculation to that above,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r_{\gamma}})\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{j\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{j})^{2}(g_{0}\wedge g_{1})\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\psi_{0}-\psi_{1})^{2}(g_{0}\wedge g_{1})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\min\Bigl(\frac{L^{2}}{n^{\gamma}},\,1\Bigr).

For general
L,r>0
, let
r^{\prime\prime}:=r\wedge(r_{\gamma}L^{2}/L_{\gamma}^{2})
and
\lambda:=(r^{\prime\prime}/r_{\gamma})^{1/2}
. Then
L/\lambda\geq L_{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}\supseteq\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r^{\prime\prime}}=\bigl\{x\mapsto\lambda f_{0}(\lambda x):f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,\lambda^{-1}L,r_{\gamma}}\bigr\}
, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,\lambda^{-1}L,r_{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\lambda^{2}\min\biggl\{\frac{L^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\biggl(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\gamma},\,1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\min\biggl\{L^{2}\biggl(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\gamma},\,\bar{r}\biggr\},

(65)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,\lambda^{-1}L,r_{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\min\Bigl\{\frac{L^{2}}{n^{\gamma}},\,\bar{r}\Bigr\}.

(66)

On the other hand, let
\breve{r}:=r\wedge(2^{1-\gamma}L^{2})
and
\lambda:=(\breve{r}/2)^{1/2}
. Then
a:=(L/\lambda)^{1/\gamma}\geq\sqrt{2}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r}\supseteq\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,\breve{r}}=\bigl\{x\mapsto\lambda f_{0}(\lambda x):f_{0}\in\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2}\bigr\}
, so by Lemma
18
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})\geq\lambda^{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8e}\min\biggl(\Bigl\{\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{2\lambda^{2/\gamma}n}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}^{1/3},\,1\biggr)

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\bar{r}\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\Bigr)^{1/3},\,1\biggr\}.

(67)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})\geq\lambda^{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,a^{\gamma},2})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\bar{r}\min\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)^{1/3},\,1\Bigr\}.

(68)

Finally, taking the maximum of the bounds in (
65
) and (
67
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma}\max\biggl\{\bar{r}\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{1/3},\,\bar{r}\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\gamma}\biggr\}\wedge\bar{r}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}},\,1\biggr\},

and similarly combining (
66
) and (
68
) yields

\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{\gamma,L,r})\gtrsim_{\gamma}\max\biggl\{\bar{r}\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)^{1/3},\,\frac{L^{2}}{n^{\gamma}}\biggr\}\wedge\bar{r}=\bar{r}\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{L^{2/\gamma}}{\bar{r}^{1/\gamma}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)^{\gamma\wedge\frac{1}{3}},\,1\biggr\}.\qed

A.2.2
Proofs for Section
2.3

Turning now to the proof of Theorem
9
, we remark that the construction in the case
\beta=1
case is very similar to that in Lemma
18
.

Proof of Theorem
9
.

Let
\tilde{L}\geq 1
. Given
h\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})
, let

m:=\Bigl\lceil\Bigl(\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}h}\Bigr)^{\beta-1}\Bigr\rceil,

so that

\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}=\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}h}\Bigr)^{\beta-1}\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}\biggl(\Bigl(\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}h}\Bigr)^{\beta-1}+1\biggr)\leq 2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}.

(69)

Noting that
m=1
if and only if
\beta=1
, define the intervals

I_{j}:=\begin{cases}(\tilde{L}^{1/\beta},\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+h)&\;\;\text{if }j=1\\
\biggl(\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+\Bigl\{\Bigl(\dfrac{j}{2}\Bigr)^{1/(\beta-1)}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigr\}2h,\;\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+\Bigl(\dfrac{j}{2}\Bigr)^{1/(\beta-1)}2h\biggr)&\;\;\text{if }j\in\{2,\dotsc,m\},\end{cases}

which are of length
h
and disjoint because
\max\bigl\{1,\bigl((j-1)/2\bigr)^{1/(\beta-1)}+1/2\bigr\}\leq(j/2)^{1/(\beta-1)}
for
j\geq 2
and
\beta\in(1,2]
. Moreover, when
m=1
, we have
\sup I_{m}-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}=h\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}
. If instead
m\geq 2
, then
\beta>1
and by (
69
),

\sup I_{m}-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}=\Bigl(\frac{m}{2}\Bigr)^{1/(\beta-1)}2h\leq\frac{2}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}.

(70)

Figure 4:
Plots of the functions
\tau
,
\Delta
and
\varphi
on the interval
[0,h]
.

Next, let

\tau:=\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h/4]}-\mathbbm{1}_{(h/4,3h/4]}+\mathbbm{1}_{(3h/4,h]}=\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h]}-2\mathbbm{1}_{(h/4,3h/4]}.

Defining
\Delta,\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\Delta(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\tau
and
\varphi(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\Delta
(see Figure
4
), we have
\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h]}(x)+\zeta\tau(x)\geq 0
for
x\in[0,h]
and
\zeta\in\{0,1\}
, and
\Delta(x)=\varphi(x)=0
for
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[0,h]
. For
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\dotsc,\zeta_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, define
\psi_{\zeta}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
to be the decreasing continuous function with
\psi_{\zeta}(0):=0
and right derivative given by

\psi_{\zeta}^{(\mathrm{R})}(x):=\begin{cases}-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h]}+\zeta_{j}\tau)(x-\inf I_{j})&\text{for }x\geq 0\\
\psi_{\zeta}^{(\mathrm{R})}(-x)&\text{for }x<0.\end{cases}

Then
\psi_{\zeta}^{(\mathrm{R})}(x)=0
whenever
|x|\notin\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\,I_{j}=:E_{m}
, and

\psi_{(0,\ldots,0)}(x)=-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}\int_{0}^{x}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{m}}

for
x\geq 0
, as illustrated in Figure
5
. Moreover, every
\psi_{\zeta}
is an odd decreasing function with

\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\begin{cases}\psi_{(0,\ldots,0)}(x)-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\zeta_{j}\Delta(x-\inf I_{j})\;\;&\text{for }x\in[0,\sup I_{m}]\\[3.0pt]
-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m\;\;&\text{for }x>\sup I_{m}\end{cases}

and antiderivative
\phi_{\zeta}\colon\mathbb{R}\to(-\infty,0]
given by
\phi_{\zeta}(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{\zeta}
, which is therefore symmetric and concave. Since
\psi_{\zeta}(x)\leq 0
for
x\geq 0
with equality when
|x|\leq\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}
, and
\psi_{\zeta}(x)\geq-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m
for
x\geq 0
with equality when
x\geq\sup I_{m}
, we have

\displaystyle-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(|x|-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})_{+}\leq\phi_{\zeta}(x)\leq-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(|x|-\sup I_{m})_{+}

(71)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, by (
69
) and (
70
),
C_{\zeta}:=\log\bigl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{\zeta}}\bigr)
satisfies

\displaystyle 2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+\frac{2}{\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(|x|-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq e^{C_{\zeta}}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(|x|-\sup I_{m})_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\Bigl(\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+\frac{2}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m}\Bigr)\leq 2\Bigl(\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+\frac{3}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\Bigr)\leq 8\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}.

(72)

Together with (
70
), this implies that
f_{\zeta}:=e^{\phi_{\zeta}-C_{\zeta}}
is a symmetric log-concave density with

\displaystyle i(f_{\zeta})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{\zeta}^{2}f_{\zeta}\leq\frac{2(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m)^{2}}{e^{C_{\zeta}}}\int_{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{\zeta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m)^{2}}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\int_{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(x-\sup I_{m})_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m)^{2}}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\Bigl(\frac{2}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m}\Bigr)\leq 10.

(73)

Moreover, if
|x|\leq\sup I_{m}
, then by (
71
),

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-4}}{8\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\leq\frac{\exp\bigl(-\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m(\sup I_{m}-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})\bigr)}{8\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigl(\phi_{\zeta}(\sup I_{m})-C_{\zeta}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq f_{\zeta}(x)\leq\frac{1}{e^{C_{\zeta}}}\leq\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}.

(74)

Figure 5:
Illustration of the functions
\psi_{\zeta}
defined in the proof of Theorem
9
for various values of
\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.

We now verify that
f_{\zeta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},r}
with
r=10
for every
\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}
. Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
. First, if
\beta=1
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, then
|\psi_{\zeta}(x+t)-\psi_{\zeta}(x)|\leq 2\tilde{L}
. On the other hand, if
\beta\in(1,2]
and
t\in[0,h]
, then

|\psi_{\zeta}(x+t)-\psi_{\zeta}(x)|\leq 2\tilde{L}h^{-(2-\beta)}t\leq 2\tilde{L}t^{\beta-1}.

Further, for
j\in[m]
, we have

\sup_{x\in I_{j}}\psi_{\zeta}(x)-\inf_{x\in I_{j}}\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\psi_{\zeta}(\inf I_{j})-\psi_{\zeta}(\sup I_{j})=\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}

and
\inf I_{j+1}-\sup I_{j}
is increasing in
j\in[m-1]
. Since
\psi_{\zeta}^{(\mathrm{R})}=0
on
\bigl(E_{m}\cup(-E_{m})\bigr)^{c}
, it follows that for
t>h
, we have

\displaystyle|\psi_{\zeta}(x+t)-\psi_{\zeta}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\{I_{j}\cap(x,x+t)\neq\emptyset\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{(-I_{j})\cap(x,x+t)\neq\emptyset\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}\biggl\{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\{I_{j}\cap(\tilde{L}^{1/\beta},\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}+t)\neq\emptyset\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{(-I_{j})\cap(-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}-t,-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})\neq\emptyset\}}\bigr)+2\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}\Bigl(\frac{4(t+h)^{\beta-1}}{(2h)^{\beta-1}}+2\Bigr)\leq\tilde{L}t^{\beta-1}(4+2)=6\tilde{L}t^{\beta-1}.

Since
f_{\zeta}
is symmetric and (
73
) holds, we have
f_{\zeta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10}
, as claimed.

For
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\dotsc,\zeta_{m})
and
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, let
S:=\{j\in[m]:\zeta_{j}\neq\xi_{j}\}
. Since
\Delta(x)=0
for
x\geq h
and
\int_{0}^{h}\Delta=0
, we have
\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\psi_{\xi}(x)
and
\phi_{\zeta}(x)=\phi_{\xi}(x)
for all
x\in[0,\infty)\setminus\bigcup_{j\in S}I_{j}
. For
j\in S
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(\psi_{\zeta}-\psi_{\xi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2})^{2}\int_{0}^{h}\Delta^{2}=\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{48},

(75)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2})^{2}\int_{0}^{h}\varphi^{2}=\frac{23\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta+1}}{15360}.

(76)

Moreover, since
|\log(x)-\log(x^{\prime})|\leq|x-x^{\prime}|
for
x,x^{\prime}\geq 1
and
|e^{x}-e^{x^{\prime}}|\leq|x-x^{\prime}|
for
x,x^{\prime}\leq 0
, (
72
) and (
69
) imply that

\displaystyle|C_{\zeta}-C_{\xi}|

\displaystyle=\bigl|\log\bigl(e^{C_{\zeta}}/(2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})\bigr)-\log\bigl(e^{C_{\xi}}/(2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta})\bigr)\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|e^{\phi_{\zeta}}-e^{\phi_{\xi}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\sum_{j\in S}\int_{I_{j}}|\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}|=\frac{|S|\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\int_{0}^{h}\varphi=\frac{|S|\tilde{L}^{1-1/\beta}h^{\beta+1}}{32}

(77)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m\tilde{L}^{1-1/\beta}h^{\beta+1}}{32}\leq\frac{h^{2}}{16}\leq\frac{\tilde{L}^{-2/\beta}}{16}\leq\frac{1}{16}.

Since
x\mapsto(e^{x}-1)/x
is increasing on
(0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)\setminus\cup_{j\in S}I_{j}}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,\infty)\setminus\cup_{j\in S}I_{j}}(e^{C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle\leq 16^{2}(e^{1/16}-1)^{2}(C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\int_{(0,\infty)\setminus\cup_{j\in S}I_{j}}f_{\xi}.

By (
71
),
|\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}|\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}m\,(\sup I_{m}-L^{1/\beta})+1/16\leq 65/16
on
E_{m}
, so for
j\in S
, it follows from (
74
) and (
76
) that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\frac{16(e^{65/16}-1)}{65}\biggr)^{2}\int_{I_{j}}(\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle<396\int_{I_{j}}\bigl((\phi_{\xi}-\phi_{\zeta})^{2}+(C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\bigr)\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle\leq 396\,\biggl(\frac{23\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta+1}}{15360\cdot 2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}+(C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\int_{I_{j}}f_{\xi}\biggr).

(78)

Let
P_{\zeta},P_{\xi}
denote the probability measures with densities
f_{\zeta},f_{\xi}
respectively. Then by (
77
) and (
78
),

\displaystyle\KL(P_{\zeta},P_{\xi})\leq\chi^{2}(P_{\zeta},P_{\xi})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{f_{\zeta}}{f_{\xi}}-1\Bigr)^{2}f_{\xi}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle<\frac{396}{2^{8}}\bigl(|S|\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}+|S|^{2}\tilde{L}^{2-2/\beta}h^{2\beta+2}\bigr).

(79)

In particular, if
|S|=1
, in which case we write
\zeta\sim\xi
(and
\zeta\sim_{j}\xi
if
S=\{j\}
for
j\in[m]
), then by Pinsker’s inequality,

\displaystyle\TV^{2}(P_{\zeta}^{\otimes n},P_{\xi}^{\otimes n})\leq\frac{n\KL(P_{\zeta},P_{\xi})}{2}<\frac{396}{2^{9}}\,n\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}(1+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}h)\leq\frac{396}{2^{8}}\,n\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}.

(80)

We now establish two separate lower bounds on the minimax
(1-\delta)
