th quantile via different choices of
h
and techniques from
53
. First, let

h:=\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggl(\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\wedge 1\biggr),

so that
\TV(P_{\zeta}^{\otimes n},P_{\xi}^{\otimes n})\leq 1/2
by (
80
). Since
\bigl\{f_{\zeta}:\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\bigr\}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10}
, it follows from (
74
), Assouad’s lemma
(
61
, Lemma 9.7)
, (
75
), (
80
) and (
69
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\mathbb{E}_{f_{\zeta}}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\zeta})^{2}}{8e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\min_{\zeta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{m}:\zeta\sim_{j}\xi}\,\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\psi_{\zeta}-\psi_{\xi})^{2}}{8e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\Bigl(1-\max_{\zeta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{m}:\zeta\sim\xi}\TV(P_{\zeta}^{\otimes n},P_{\xi}^{\otimes n})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{m}{4\times 4e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{48}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{16e^{4}\times 48}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\geq\frac{1}{6144e^{4}}\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\wedge 1\Bigr\}.

(81)

Moreover, by (
75
) and (
69
),

\displaystyle d:=\max_{\zeta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\psi_{\zeta}-\psi_{\xi})^{2}}{8e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}=\frac{m\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{192e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\in\Bigl[\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{192e^{4}},\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{96e^{4}}\Bigr].

(82)

Hence, taking
\epsilon=1/12
and

0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{136}<\frac{\frac{1}{64}-\epsilon^{2}}{(1+\epsilon)^{2}}\leq\frac{\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{6144e^{4}}-\epsilon^{2}d}{(1+\epsilon)^{2}d}

in
53
, we deduce from (
74
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{\zeta}}\biggl(1-\delta,\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\zeta})^{2}f_{\zeta}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{\zeta}}\biggl(1-\delta,\,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\zeta})^{2}}{8e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon^{2}d\geq\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{144\times 192e^{4}}\geq\frac{1}{576\times 192e^{4}}\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\wedge 1\biggr\}.

Since the function
x\mapsto x^{3}+3x^{2}(1-x)=:H(x)
satisfies
(H\circ H\circ H\circ H)(1/4)<1/136
, taking
k=4
in
53
shows that for every
\delta\in(0,1/4]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})\geq\frac{1}{4^{4}\times 3^{2}\times 576\times 192e^{4}}\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\wedge 1\Bigr\}.

(83)

Now for
\delta\in(0,1/4]
, we instead define

h:=\Bigl\{\frac{2^{7}}{1188n\tilde{L}}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\Bigr\}^{1/(\beta+2)}\wedge\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}.

Fix
\zeta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{m}
with
S=[m]
. Then by (
79
) and (
69
),

\displaystyle\KL(P_{\zeta}^{\otimes n},P_{\xi}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle<\frac{396}{2^{8}}\,n\bigl(m\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}+m^{2}\tilde{L}^{2-2/\beta}h^{2\beta+2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{396n}{2^{8}}(2\tilde{L}h^{\beta+2}+4h^{4})\leq\frac{396n\tilde{L}h^{\beta+2}}{2^{7}}(1+2\tilde{L}^{-2/\beta})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1188n\tilde{L}h^{\beta+2}}{2^{7}}\leq\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr).

Arguing as in (
81
), we deduce from
53
and (
82
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\theta\in\{\zeta,\xi\}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{\theta}}\biggl(1-\delta,\,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\theta})^{2}}{8e^{4}\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta}}{192e^{4}}\geq\frac{1}{768e^{4}}\biggl(\biggl\{\frac{2^{7}\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{1188n}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\biggr\}^{\beta/(\beta+2)}\wedge 1\biggr).

(84)

Finally, for general
L,r>0
and
s:=r\wedge 10(L/6)^{2/\beta}
, we have
\tilde{L}:=L(s/10)^{-\beta/2}/6\geq 1
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,s}=\bigl\{x\mapsto(s/10)^{1/2}f_{0}\bigl((s/10)^{1/2}x\bigr):f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10}\bigr\}
, it follows from (
81
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})=\frac{s}{10}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{s}{10\times 6144e^{4}}\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{(L/6)^{2/\beta}}{ns/10}\Bigr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\wedge 1\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)},\,1\biggr\}.

(85)

Moreover, from (
83
), for
\delta\in(0,1/4]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})=\frac{s}{10}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,6\tilde{L},10})

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)},\,1\biggr\}.

(86)

Similarly, it follows from (
84
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})\gtrsim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\beta/(\beta+2)},\,1\biggr\}.

(87)

The desired lower bounds on
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})
and
\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L,r})
follow respectively from (
85
), and the maximum of the bounds in (
86
) and (
87
).
∎

We conclude this section with a minimax lower bound over the larger class of densities with Hölder scores that are not necessarily decreasing. For
\beta\in[1,2]
and
L>0
, denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L}

the set of all locally absolutely continuous densities
f_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}
whose score functions
\psi_{0}=(\log f_{0})^{\prime}
(defined Lebesgue almost everywhere when
\beta=1
) satisfy

|\psi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(y)|\leq L|x-y|^{\beta-1}

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. For
r>0
, define
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r}:=\{f_{0}\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L}:i(f_{0})\leq r\}
.

Proposition 19
.

For
\beta\in[1,2]
,
L,r>0
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta\in(0,1/4)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}}+\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}},\,1\biggr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r})

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\beta}r^{\prime}\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{r^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}},\,1\biggr\},

where
r^{\prime}:=r\wedge L^{2/\beta}
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
\tilde{L}:=2L/5\geq 1
. Let
a:=\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}
and define
\psi_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\psi_{0}(x):=-\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}\int_{0}^{x}\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\leq|z|-a\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}\}}\,\mathrm{d}z=-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\sgn(x)\min\bigl\{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a)_{+},1\bigr\}.

Then for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

|\psi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(y)|\leq\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}(|x-y|\wedge 2\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})\leq 2^{2-\beta}\tilde{L}|x-y|^{\beta-1}.

(88)

Given
h\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/2]
, let

\tau:=\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h/4]}-\mathbbm{1}_{(h/4,3h/4]}+\mathbbm{1}_{(3h/4,h]}=\mathbbm{1}_{(0,h]}-2\mathbbm{1}_{(h/4,3h/4]},

and as in the proof of Theorem
9
, define
\Delta,\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\Delta(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\tau
and
\varphi(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\Delta
(see Figure
4
). Then
\Delta(x)=\varphi(x)=0
for
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[0,h]
. Let
m:=\lfloor\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/(2h)\rfloor\geq 1
, and for
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\dotsc,\zeta_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, define
\Delta_{\zeta}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\Delta_{\zeta}(x):=\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\zeta_{j}\Delta\Bigl(x-a-\frac{\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}}{2}-(j-1)h\Bigr)=:-\Delta_{\zeta}(-x)

for
x\geq 0
. Since
mh\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/2
, we have
\Delta_{\zeta}(x)=0
whenever
|x|\leq\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}
or
|x|\geq a+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}
. Moreover,
|\Delta_{\zeta}(x)|\leq\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}/4
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, so

|\Delta_{\zeta}(x)-\Delta_{\zeta}(y)|\leq\bigl(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2}|x-y|\bigr)\wedge\frac{\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1}}{2}\leq 2^{\beta-2}\tilde{L}|x-y|^{\beta-1}

(89)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Now let

\psi_{\zeta}:=\psi_{0}+\Delta_{\zeta}.

Then by (
88
) and (
89
),

|\psi_{\zeta}(x)-\psi_{\zeta}(y)|\leq(2^{2-\beta}+2^{\beta-2})\tilde{L}|x-y|^{\beta-1}\leq L|x-y|^{\beta-1}

(90)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover,
\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\psi_{0}(x)=0
when
|x|\leq a
and
\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\psi_{0}(x)=-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\sgn(x)
when
|x|\geq a+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}
. Since
h\in(0,\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/2]
, we have
\psi_{\zeta}(x)\leq(-\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}+\frac{1}{4}\tilde{L}h^{\beta-1})_{+}=0
for all
x\geq 0
.
Therefore, the functions
\phi_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to(-\infty,0]
and
\phi_{\zeta}\colon\mathbb{R}\to(-\infty,0]
given by
\phi_{0}(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{\zeta}
and
\phi_{\zeta}(x):=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{\zeta}
respectively are symmetric, and since
\int_{0}^{x}\Delta_{\zeta}\geq 0
for all
x\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a)_{+}\leq\phi_{0}(x)\leq\phi_{\zeta}(x)\leq-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a-\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})_{+}\leq 0

(91)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
a=\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\geq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}
, this means that
C_{\zeta}:=\log\bigl(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{\zeta}}\bigr)
satisfies

\displaystyle 2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}<2(a+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a)_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq e^{C_{\zeta}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a-\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2(a+2\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})\leq 6\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}.

(92)

Thus,
f_{\zeta}:=e^{\phi_{\zeta}-C_{\zeta}}
is a symmetric, absolutely continuous density, and for all
x\in[-(a+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}),a+\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigl(-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(|x|-a)_{+}-C_{\zeta}\bigr)\leq f_{\zeta}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}.

(93)

Further, by (
91
) and (
92
),

\displaystyle i(f_{\zeta})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi_{\zeta}^{2}f_{\zeta}\leq\frac{2\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{e^{C_{\zeta}}}\int_{a}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{\zeta}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\int_{a}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}(x-a-\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta})_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}x=2.

Together with (
90
), this implies that
f_{\zeta}\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,2}
for all
\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.

We now proceed with calculations similar to those in the proof of Theorem
9
. For
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\dotsc,\zeta_{m})
and
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, let
S:=\{j\in[m]:\zeta_{j}\neq\xi_{j}\}
. Define
I_{j}:=\bigl[a+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}+(j-1)h,a+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}+jh\bigr]
for
j\in[m].
Since
\Delta(x)=0
for
x\geq h
and
\int_{0}^{h}\Delta=0
, we have
\psi_{\zeta}(x)=\psi_{\xi}(x)
and
\phi_{\zeta}(x)=\phi_{\xi}(x)
for all
x\in[0,\infty)\setminus\bigcup_{j\in S}I_{j}
. For
j\in S
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(\psi_{\zeta}-\psi_{\xi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2})^{2}\int_{0}^{h}\Delta^{2}=\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{48},

(94)

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{L}h^{\beta-2})^{2}\int_{0}^{h}\varphi^{2}=\frac{23\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta+1}}{15360}.

In view of (
92
), (
93
) and the facts that
h\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/2\leq 1/2
,
m\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/(2h)
and
|\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}|\leq\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\cdot\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}
= 1 on
\mathbb{R}
, virtually identical arguments to those in (
77
)–(
78
) yield the bounds

\displaystyle|C_{\zeta}-C_{\xi}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|S|\tilde{L}^{1-1/\beta}h^{\beta+1}}{32}\leq\frac{\tilde{L}^{1-2/\beta}h^{\beta}}{64}\leq\frac{1}{16},

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)\setminus\cup_{j\in S}I_{j}}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle\leq 16^{2}(e^{1/16}-1)^{2}(C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\int_{(0,\infty)\setminus\cup_{j\in S}I_{j}}f_{\xi},

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle<396\,\biggl(\frac{23\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta+1}}{15360\cdot 2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}+(C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta})^{2}\int_{I_{j}}f_{\xi}\biggr)

for
j\in S
. Thus, denoting by
P_{\zeta},P_{\xi}
the probability measures with densities
f_{\zeta},f_{\xi}
respectively,

\displaystyle\KL(P_{\zeta},P_{\xi})\leq\chi^{2}(P_{\zeta},P_{\xi})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{f_{\zeta}}{f_{\xi}}-1\Bigr)^{2}f_{\xi}=2\int_{0}^{\infty}(e^{\phi_{\zeta}-\phi_{\xi}+C_{\xi}-C_{\zeta}}-1)^{2}\,f_{\xi}

\displaystyle<\frac{396}{2^{8}}\bigl(|S|\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}+|S|^{2}\tilde{L}^{2-2/\beta}h^{2\beta+2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{396m\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}}{2^{8}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\tilde{L}^{-2/\beta}}{2}\Bigr)\leq\frac{1188m\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}}{2^{9}},

(95)

where the final two inequalities hold because
m\leq\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/(2h)
and
\tilde{L}\geq 1
. Now let

\displaystyle h:=\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggl(\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{113n}\Bigr)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\wedge\frac{1}{2}\biggr),\qquad M:=\lceil e^{m/8}\rceil\geq 2,

where we recall that
m=\lfloor\tilde{L}^{-1/\beta}/(2h)\rfloor
. By the Gilbert–Varshamov lemma
(
61
, Exercise 8.10)
, there exist distinct
\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{M}\in\{0,1\}^{m}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\zeta_{j}^{p}\neq\zeta_{j}^{q}\}}>m/4
for any distinct
p,q\in[M]

By (
95
),

\displaystyle\frac{M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\KL(P_{\zeta^{j}}^{\otimes n},P_{\zeta^{1}}^{\otimes n})+\log(2-M^{-1})}{\log M}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m/8}\cdot\frac{1188mn\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}}{2^{9}}+\frac{\log(3/2)}{\log 2}<\frac{3}{4}.

We conclude that if
L\geq 5/2
, then by (
93
), Fano’s lemma
(
61
, Corollary 8.12)
and (
94
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,2})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\ell\in[M]}\,\mathbb{E}_{f_{\zeta^{\ell}}}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\zeta^{\ell}})^{2}}{6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)\geq\frac{2m}{4}\cdot\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{2^{2}\times 6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\times 48}\Bigl(1-\frac{3}{4}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tilde{L}^{2-\frac{2}{\beta}}h^{2(\beta-1)}}{2^{6}\times 6e\times 48}\gtrsim\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}},\,1\biggr\}.

(96)

Similarly, for
\delta\in(0,1/4)
,
53
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,2})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\ell\in[M]}\,\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{\zeta^{\ell}}}\biggl(1-\delta,\,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\zeta^{\ell}})^{2}}{6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2m}{4}\cdot\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{2^{2}\times 6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\times 48}\gtrsim\min\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}}{n}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}},\,1\biggr\}.

(97)

Now suppose instead that we define

h:=\biggl\{\frac{2^{10}}{1188n\tilde{L}^{2(1-\frac{1}{\beta})}}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr)\biggr\}^{1/(2\beta)}\wedge\frac{1}{2\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}.

Fix
\zeta,\xi\in\{0,1\}^{m}
with
S=[m]
. Then by (
95
),

\displaystyle\KL(P_{\zeta}^{\otimes n},P_{\xi}^{\otimes n})<\frac{1188mn\tilde{L}^{2-1/\beta}h^{2\beta+1}}{2^{9}}\leq\frac{1188n\tilde{L}^{2-\frac{2}{\beta}}h^{2\beta}}{2^{10}}\leq\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\Bigr),

so by (
93
) and
53
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,2})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{n}\in\hat{\Psi}_{n}}\max_{\theta\in\{\zeta,\xi\}}\mathrm{Quantile}_{f_{\theta}}\biggl(1-\delta,\,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int_{(-I_{j})\cup I_{j}}\frac{(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{\theta})^{2}}{6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq 2m\,\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}h^{2\beta-1}}{2^{2}\times 6e\tilde{L}^{1/\beta}\times 48}\gtrsim\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\tilde{L}^{2/\beta}\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}},\,1\biggr\}.

(98)

Finally, consider arbitrary
L,r>0
. Let
s:=\min\bigl\{r,2(2L/5)^{2/\beta}\bigr\}
. Then
L(s/2)^{-\beta/2}\geq 5/2
and

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r}\supseteq\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,s}=\bigl\{(s/2)^{1/2}f_{0}\bigl((s/2)^{1/2}\cdot\bigr):f_{0}\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L(s/2)^{-\beta/2},2}\bigr\},

so by (
96
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{s\mathcal{M}_{n}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L(s/2)^{-\beta/2},2})}{2}\gtrsim s\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}},\,1\biggr\}.

Similarly, by (
97
) and (
98
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L,r})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{s\mathcal{M}_{n}(\delta,\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,L(s/2)^{-\beta/2},2})}{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim s\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{2(\beta-1)}{2\beta+1}}+\biggl(\frac{L^{2/\beta}}{s}\cdot\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}},\,1\biggr\},

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs for Section
3

Proof of Lemma
10
.

By Fubini’s theorem,
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{h}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,f_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=1
. Moreover, for every
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
0\leq f_{h}(x_{0})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}(x_{0}-y)f_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq 1/h<\infty
, so

\displaystyle f_{h}(x_{0})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{x_{0}}K_{h}^{\prime}(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,f_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{-\infty}^{x_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}^{\prime}(x-y)f_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x.

This shows that
f_{h}
is an absolutely continuous function on
\mathbb{R}
. Since
|K_{h}^{\prime}|\leq h^{-2}
almost everywhere on
\mathbb{R}
, it follows from the dominated convergence theorem that

(K_{h}^{\prime}*f_{0})(x^{\prime})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}^{\prime}(x^{\prime}-y)f_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\to(K_{h}^{\prime}*f_{0})(x_{0})

as
x^{\prime}\to x_{0}
, so
K_{h}^{\prime}*f_{0}
is continuous. Hence, by the fundamental theorem of calculus,
f_{h}
is in fact continuously differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
with derivative
f_{h}^{\prime}=K_{h}^{\prime}*f_{0}
.

Moreover,
f_{0}
is locally absolutely continuous by assumption, so
f_{0}(x)-f_{0}(0)=\int_{0}^{x}f_{0}^{\prime}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}^{\prime}=0
, it follows again by Fubini’s theorem that

\displaystyle f_{h}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}^{\prime}(x-y)\bigl(f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(0)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}^{\prime}(x-y)f_{0}^{\prime}(z)(\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\leq z\leq y\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{y<z\leq 0\}})\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}(x-z)f_{0}^{\prime}(z)(\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\geq 0\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{z<0\}})\,\mathrm{d}z=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}K_{h}(x-z)f_{0}^{\prime}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

Thus,
f_{h}^{\prime}=K_{h}*f_{0}^{\prime}
.

If in addition
f_{0}
is log-concave, then
\psi_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[-\infty,\infty]
is decreasing. Since
K_{h}
is supported on
[-h,h]
, it follows that for
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\omega f_{h}^{\prime}(x)=\omega\int_{x-h}^{x+h}K_{h}(x-z)\psi_{0}(z)f_{0}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\omega\int_{x-h}^{x+h}K_{h}(x-z)\psi_{0}(x+\omega h)f_{0}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\omega\psi_{0}(x+\omega h)f_{h}(x).

This implies that
\omega\psi_{h}(x)\geq\omega\psi_{0}(x+\omega h)
when
f_{h}(x)>0
, i.e.
[x-h,x+h]\cap\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})\neq\emptyset
. Otherwise, either
x+h\leq\inf\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})
, in which case
\omega\psi_{0}(x+\omega h)=\psi_{h}(x)=\infty
, or
x-h\geq\sup\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})
and
\omega\psi_{0}(x+\omega h)=\psi_{h}(x)=-\infty
. Therefore, (
12
) holds in all cases.
∎

The next two results apply to any Borel probability distribution
P_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}
and
X_{1},\dotsc,X_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P_{0}
with empirical distribution
\mathbb{P}_{n}
. Some of the remaining lemmas require
P_{0}
to have an absolutely continuous density, but the log-concavity assumption will only be needed again in the proofs of Propositions
11
and
12
.

First, we establish a simultaneous concentration bound for indicator functions of intervals. It is convenient to define
\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p,q):=\mathbbm{1}_{\{p>q\}}\,\mathrm{kl}(p,q)
for
p,q\in[0,1]
, where
\mathrm{kl}
is as in (
16
). For a related result, see
60
.

Lemma 20
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
,
we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta})\geq 1-\delta
.

Proof.

For each interval
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, define
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A):=(n/2)\,\mathbb{P}_{n}(A)-(n/2-1)P_{0}(A)
. If
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)\in[0,1]
for some interval
A
, then (
17
) holds with the infimal value of zero attained at
t=\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)
. In particular, for
n\leq 2
, the claim (
17
) holds with probability 1 for all intervals
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, we suppose henceforth that
n\geq 3
. For
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(x_{0},x_{1}):=\begin{cases}[x_{0},x_{1}]&\text{ if }x_{0}\leq x_{1}\\
\mathbb{R}\setminus(x_{1},x_{0})&\text{ if }x_{1}<x_{0}.\end{cases}

Letting
\mathbb{P}_{n-2}(B):=(n-2)^{-1}\,\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{i}\in B\}}
for Borel sets
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, we apply Lemma
27
to the

n-2
conditionally independent and identically distributed Bernoulli random variables
(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{i}\in\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}})_{i\in[n-2]}
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl\{\mathrm{kl}_{+}\Bigl(\mathbb{P}_{n-2}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\},P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}\Bigr)\geq\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n-2}\Bigm|X_{n-1},X_{n}\Bigr\}\leq\frac{\delta}{n^{2}}.

Moreover, by Lemma
29
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\mathbb{P}_{n-2}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}+\frac{2}{n},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{n-2}{n}\,\mathrm{kl}_{+}\Bigl(\mathbb{P}_{n-2}\,\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\},P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}\Bigr).

Hence, by the law of total expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{n-1},X_{n})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)\geq\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n}\biggr\}\leq\frac{\delta}{n^{2}},

so by symmetry and a union bound, the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}\biggl\{\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i},X_{j})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i},X_{j})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)<\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n}\biggr\}

has probability at least
1-\delta
.

Now suppose that
\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta}
holds and let
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be an interval. As noted, if
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)\in[0,1]
, then (
17
) follows. Now suppose that
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)>1
. Then since
\mathbb{P}_{n}(A)>2/n
, we may choose a random tuple
(i_{A},j_{A})\in[n]^{2}
such that
i_{A}<j_{A}
, with
\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}},X_{j_{A}})\subseteq A
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}(A)=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}},X_{j_{A}})\}
, so that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta}
we have

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\,

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(A)+\frac{2t}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}(A)+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}},X_{j_{A}})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}(A)+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}},X_{j_{A}})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}},X_{j_{A}})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)<\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n},

where we have used the fact that
q\mapsto\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p,q)
is decreasing on
[0,1]
for each
p\in[0,1]
. Therefore, (
17
) holds when
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)>1
. Finally, if
\mathbb{D}_{n}(A)<0
, then we may proceed similarly with
\mathbb{R}\setminus A
in place of
A
. Indeed, since
\mathbb{P}_{n}(A)<1
, we may choose a random tuple
(i_{A}^{\circ},j_{A}^{\circ})\in[n]^{2}
with
i_{A}^{\circ}>j_{A}^{\circ}
such that
\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}^{\circ}},X_{j_{A}^{\circ}})\subseteq\mathbb{R}\setminus A
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}^{\circ}},X_{j_{A}^{\circ}})\}=\mathbb{P}_{n}(\mathbb{R}\setminus A)
. Then on the event
\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\,

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(A)+\frac{2t}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}(A)\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(1-\mathbb{P}_{n}(A),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\{1-P_{0}(A)\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{kl}_{+}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}^{\circ}},X_{j_{A}^{\circ}})\},\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}\{\mathcal{I}(X_{i_{A}^{\circ}},X_{j_{A}^{\circ}})\}+\frac{2}{n}\biggr)<\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n},

so (
17
) again holds. Thus,
\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, so
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta})\geq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{n,\delta})\geq 1-\delta
.
∎

Recall that
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is
unimodal
if there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\varphi(x)\leq\varphi(x^{\prime})
for
x\leq x^{\prime}\leq x_{0}
and
\varphi(x)\geq\varphi(x^{\prime})
for
x_{0}\leq x\leq x^{\prime}
. We now extend Lemma
20
from the class of indicator functions of intervals to the class
\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
of all unimodal functions
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
. For
n\geq 3
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, let

\displaystyle\tilde{P}^{\omega}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr){P_{0}}(\varphi)+\frac{1+\omega}{n}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})\tilde{P}^{\omega}(\varphi)+3\epsilon_{n,\delta}+3\omega\sqrt{4\epsilon_{n,\delta}\tilde{P}^{1}(\varphi)\{1-\tilde{P}^{-1}(\varphi)\}+\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{2}}}{2(1+\epsilon_{n,\delta})}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)+3\epsilon_{n,\delta}+3\omega\sqrt{\epsilon_{n,\delta}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\{1-\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\}+\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{2}}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}+\frac{\omega-1}{n-2}.

Lemma 21
.

Suppose that
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
holds. Then

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{2t}{n}\biggr)<\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n}

for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
. Further, if
n\geq 3
, then for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
we have
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\leq 2P_{0}(\varphi)+21\epsilon_{n,\delta}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\in[\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-1}(\varphi),\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)]
and
P_{0}(\varphi)\in[\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-1}(\varphi),\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)]
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\max_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\bigl|\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi)-P_{0}(\varphi)\bigr|\leq 26\sqrt{\epsilon_{n,\delta}P_{0}(\varphi)}+112\epsilon_{n,\delta}

for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
. For each
\gamma\in[0,1]
, the set
A_{\gamma}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\varphi(x)\geq\gamma\}
is an interval, and moreover for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\varphi(x)=\int_{0}^{\varphi(x)}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\gamma}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma.

In addition, let

\tau_{\gamma}(\varphi):=\argmin_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A_{\gamma}),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(A_{\gamma})+\frac{2t}{n}\biggr).

Then by Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)

\displaystyle,\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{2t}{n}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\Bigr),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\Bigr)+\frac{2}{n}\Bigl(\int_{0}^{1}\tau_{\gamma}(\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\Bigr)\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}_{n}(A_{\gamma})\,\mathrm{d}\gamma,\int_{0}^{1}\Bigl\{\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(A_{\gamma})+\frac{2\tau_{\gamma}(\varphi)}{n}\Bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\mathbb{P}_{n}(A_{\gamma}),\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)\,P_{0}(A_{\gamma})+\frac{2\tau_{\gamma}(\varphi)}{n}\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma<\frac{\log(n^{2}/\delta)}{n}=\frac{9\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{2},

where the penultimate inequality follows from the joint convexity of the Kullback–Leibler divergence (Lemma
28
) and the final inequality follows from the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
in (
17
).

For the second part of the lemma,
taking

p=\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\quad\text{and}\quad q=\Bigl(1-\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{2}{n}\int_{0}^{1}\tau_{\gamma}(\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\in[\tilde{P}^{-1}(\varphi),\tilde{P}^{1}(\varphi)]

in the second part of Lemma
30
yields

\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\in[\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-1}(\varphi),\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)]
. Since
n\geq 3
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have
\log(n^{2}/\delta)>1
, so
9\epsilon_{n,\delta}/2>1/n
and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\leq\tilde{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{2}{n}+\frac{3\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\biggl(4\epsilon_{n,\delta}\Bigl(P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{2}{n}\Bigr)+\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{21\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{2}+3\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\Bigl(P_{0}(\varphi)+\frac{21\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq 2P_{0}(\varphi)+21\epsilon_{n,\delta}.

By instead applying the third part of Lemma
30
with the same values of
p
and
q
, we obtain

P_{0}(\varphi)\in[\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-1}(\varphi),\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)]
. We have

\displaystyle\max_{\omega\in\{-1,1\}}\bigl|\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi)-P_{0}(\varphi)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,1}(\varphi)-\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-1}(\varphi)\bigr|\leq\frac{2n}{n-2}\Biggl(\frac{3\sqrt{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\epsilon_{n,\delta}+\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{2}}}{1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta}}+\frac{1}{n}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 18\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl(2P_{0}(\varphi)+22\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr)^{1/2}+27\epsilon_{n,\delta}\leq 26\sqrt{\epsilon_{n,\delta}P_{0}(\varphi)}+112\epsilon_{n,\delta},

as required.
∎

We now establish the simultaneous validity of the confidence intervals
\bigl[\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z),\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(1)}(z)\bigr]
for
\psi_{h}(z)
, across all
z\in\mathbb{R}
and bandwidths
h>0
. When
\psi_{0}
is decreasing, the multiscale aggregation in (
15
) is designed to exploit the relationship between
\psi_{h}
and
\psi_{0}
in Lemma
10
, thereby guaranteeing the coverage of the confidence band
\bigl[\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(-1)}(\cdot),\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(1)}(\cdot)\bigr]
for
\psi_{0}(\cdot)
in Proposition
11
.

Lemma 22
.

Suppose that
n\geq 3
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
P_{0}
has an absolutely continuous density
f_{0}
. Then on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-h^{2}\,f_{h}(z)\biggr\}\quad\text{and}\quad 0\leq\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z)\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z))}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-h^{2}f_{h}^{\prime}(z)\biggr\}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
h>0
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}\subseteq\bigcap_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\bigcap_{h>0}\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)\leq\psi_{h}(z)\leq\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(1)}(z)\bigr\}.

Proof.

Fix
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
h>0
. Recall that
K
is absolutely continuous on
\mathbb{R}
with a weak derivative
K^{\prime}
given by
K^{\prime}(x):=-\sgn(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|<1\}}
. Writing
(K^{\prime})_{+}
and
(K^{\prime})_{-}
for the positive and negative parts of
K^{\prime}
, observe that the functions
\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}^{\pm}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(x):=K\Bigl(\frac{z-x}{h}\Bigr)=h\,K_{h}(z-x),\qquad\varphi_{1}^{\pm}(x):=(K^{\prime})_{\pm}\Bigl(\frac{z-x}{h}\Bigr)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\in(z,z\pm h)\}}

are all unimodal, take values in
[0,1]
and are supported within
[z-h,z+h]
. Letting
\varphi_{1}:=\varphi_{1}^{+}-\varphi_{1}^{-}
, so that
\varphi_{1}(x)=\sgn(x-z)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x-z|<h\}}
, we deduce from Lemma
10
that

\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{0})=h\hat{f}_{n,h}(z),\quad P_{0}(\varphi_{0})=hf_{h}(z),\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1})=h^{2}\hat{f}_{n,h}^{\prime}(z),\quad P_{0}(\varphi_{1})=h^{2}f_{h}^{\prime}(z)

and
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{0})\vee\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1}^{-})\vee\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1}^{+})\leq\mathbb{P}_{n}([z-h,z+h])=:\hat{p}_{z,h}
. Henceforth, we work on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
. By the second part of Lemma
21
applied to
\varphi\in\{\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}^{+},\varphi_{1}^{-}\}
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\omega P_{0}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\leq\omega\cdot\frac{(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)+3\epsilon_{n,\delta}+3\omega\sqrt{\epsilon_{n,\delta}\,\bigl(\hat{p}_{z,h}+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr)}-(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-\omega)/n}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\frac{(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)+\omega\hat{V}_{n,\delta,h}(z)+(3-4/n)\epsilon_{n,\delta}-1/n}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}.

Hence, taking
\varphi=\varphi_{0}
, it follows that
\omega\bigl\{h^{2}\,f_{h}(z)\,(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)-\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)\bigr\}\leq 0
for
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
. Moreover, writing
\hat{\tau}:=\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\omega h^{2}f^{\prime}_{h}(z)

\displaystyle=\omega\,P_{0}(\varphi_{1})=\omega\{P_{0}(\varphi_{1}^{+})-P_{0}(\varphi_{1}^{-})\}

\displaystyle\leq\omega\cdot\frac{(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1}^{+})-\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1}^{-})\}+2\omega\hat{V}_{n,\delta,h}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}

\displaystyle=\omega\hat{\tau}\cdot\frac{(1-2\epsilon_{n,\delta})h^{2}\,|\hat{f}_{n,h}^{\prime}(z)|\,+2\omega\hat{\tau}\hat{V}_{n,\delta,h}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}=\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}.

If
\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)>0
, then
\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)>0
. Also, if
\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)>0
, then
\hat{f}_{n,h}(z)>0
and hence there must exist
X_{i}\in[z-h,z+h]
for some
i\in[n]
, so
f_{h}(z)>0
. Thus, when
\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)>0
and
\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)>0
, we have

\displaystyle\omega\bigl\{\psi_{h}(z)-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega f^{\prime}_{h}(z)}{f_{h}(z)}-\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\omega h^{2}f^{\prime}_{h}(z)}{h^{2}f_{h}(z)}-\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{h^{2}\,f_{h}(z)\,(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}\leq 0,

since
n>2
. Otherwise, if
\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)\leq 0
, then since
\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(1)}(z)>0
we have
\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)\geq 0
. Hence, if in addition
\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)>0
, then

\displaystyle\omega\bigl\{\psi_{h}(z)-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\,f^{\prime}_{h}(z)}{f_{h}(z)}-\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\omega\,h^{2}\,f^{\prime}_{h}(z)}{h^{2}\,f_{h}(z)}-\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{h^{2}\,f_{h}(z)\,(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}\leq 0.

Finally, if
\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)\leq 0
, then
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)=\omega\times\infty
so again
\omega\bigl\{\psi_{h}(z)-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)\bigr\}\leq 0
, which completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
11
.

The first assertion follows from Lemma
20
. Next, on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, Lemma
22
ensures that
0\leq\omega\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{h}(z)\bigr\}
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
and
h>0
. When
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
, this implies that

\displaystyle\omega\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(\omega)}(x)=\inf\bigl\{\omega\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z):\omega(x-z)\geq h>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf\{\omega\psi_{h}(z):\omega(x-z)\geq h>0\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf\{\omega\psi_{0}(z+\omega h):\omega(x-z)\geq h>0\}

\displaystyle\geq\omega\psi_{0}(x)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, where the second inequality follows from the log-concavity of
f_{0}
and Lemma
10
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}\subseteq\bigcap_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(-1)}(x)\leq\psi_{0}(x)\leq\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(1)}(x)\bigr\}=\bigcap_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{\psi_{0}(x)\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x)\bigr\},

(99)

as required.
∎

Next, on
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
, we seek to bound the widths of the confidence intervals
\bigl[\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z),\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(1)}(z)\bigr]
for
\psi_{h}(z)
, so as to obtain via (
99
) the pointwise bounds in Proposition
12
on the error of the final multiscale estimator
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}
. To this end, in Lemma
24
below, we control the widths of the confidence intervals
\hat{\mathsf{M}}_{n,\delta,h}(z),\hat{\mathsf{D}}_{n,\delta,h}(z)
for
h^{2}f_{h}^{\prime}(z),h^{2}f_{h}(z)
respectively, from which the above interval for
\psi_{h}(z)=f_{h}^{\prime}(z)/f_{h}(z)
is derived. In particular, (
100
) ultimately bounds the multiplicative error of the lower estimate for
f_{h}(z)
; see (
105
) and its application in (
107
).

Lemma 23
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
holds with
n\geq 3
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
h>0
, we have

\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}([z-h,z+h])+\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})^{2}(1-2/n)^{2}}\leq 129\bigl\{P_{0}([z-h,z+h])\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}.

Proof.

For
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
h>0
, define
\varphi_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to\{0,1\}
by
\varphi_{2}:=\mathbbm{1}_{[z-h,z+h]}
. Since
\varphi_{2}\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})\leq 2P_{0}(\varphi_{2})+21\epsilon_{n,\delta}
by Lemma
21
. Thus, using the fact that
(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)>\{1+8/(9n)\}(1-2/n)\geq 35/81
when
n\geq 3
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, we obtain

\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})+\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})^{2}(1-2/n)^{2}}\leq 11P_{0}(\varphi_{2})+118\epsilon_{n,\delta}\leq 129\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi_{2})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}.\qed

Lemma 24
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
holds with
n\geq 3
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. If
P_{0}
has an absolutely continuous density
f_{0}
, then for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
h>0
and
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-h^{2}f_{h}(z)\biggr\}\leq\Pi_{1,z,h}\cdot h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{hf_{h}(z)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2},

(100)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z)\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z))}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-h^{2}f_{h}^{\prime}(z)\biggr\}\leq\Pi_{2,z,h}\cdot\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{hf_{h}(z)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}

(101)

where
\Pi_{1,z,h}
and
\Pi_{2,z,h}
are defined in (
19
).

Proof.

For
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
h>0
, define the functions
\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}^{\pm},\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\Phi_{\mathrm{uni}}
as in Lemmas
22
and
23
. By Lemma
21
, for any
\varphi\in\{\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}^{+},\varphi_{1}^{-}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\omega^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}}\bigl|\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega^{\prime}}(\varphi)-P_{0}(\varphi)\bigr|\leq 138\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}.

(102)

For
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-h\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=3h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\}^{1/2}-\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{0})\bigl(1-\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{0})\bigr)+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq\frac{3h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}\leq 35h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi_{2})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2},

where the final inequality follows from Lemma
23
. Hence, by (
102
) and the first part of Lemma
22
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\omega\biggl\{\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-hP_{0}(\varphi_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi_{0})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}\biggl\{\frac{35\bigl(P_{0}(\varphi_{2})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr)^{1/2}}{\bigl(P_{0}(\varphi_{0})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr)^{1/2}}+138\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{1,z,h}\cdot h\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{hf_{h}(z)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2},

which establishes (
100
). Similarly, with
\hat{\tau}\equiv\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z)
,

\displaystyle\omega\,\biggl\{

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\tau}\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,\omega}(\varphi_{1}^{+})+\hat{\mathbb{J}}_{n,\delta,-\omega}(\varphi_{1}^{-})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}\sum_{\varphi\in\{\varphi_{1}^{+},\varphi_{1}^{-}\}}\Bigl(\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}-\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\bigl(1-\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi)\bigr)+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\varphi_{2})+\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}\leq 69\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi_{2})\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2},

(103)

where the final inequality follows from another application of Lemma
23
. We conclude from the second part of Lemma
22
, (
B
) and (
102
) that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\omega\,\biggl\{\frac{\hat{\tau}\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)}-P_{0}(\varphi_{1}^{+})+P_{0}(\varphi_{1}^{-})\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 374\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\max_{\varphi\in\{\varphi_{1}^{+},\varphi_{1}^{-},\varphi_{2}/2\}}\bigl\{P_{0}(\varphi)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2}=\Pi_{2,z,h}\cdot\epsilon_{n,\delta}^{1/2}\bigl\{hf_{h}(z)\vee\epsilon_{n,\delta}\bigr\}^{1/2},

as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12
.

Fix
n\geq 3
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and work throughout on the event
\mathcal{G}_{n,\delta}
. Fix
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

(a)
Let
\omega\in\{-1,1\}
. If
\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})\neq\emptyset
, then
\Delta_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})=\infty
by definition, so the result holds trivially. Otherwise, consider any
(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})
. By the log-concavity of
f_{0}
and (
12
) in Lemma
10
, we have
\omega\bigl(\psi_{h}(z)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)\leq\omega\bigl(\psi_{0}(z-\omega h)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)\leq|\psi_{0}(z-\omega h)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})|
, so

\displaystyle\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{h}(z)\bigr)+|\psi_{0}(z-\omega h)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})|.

Since
\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})\subseteq\{(z,h)\in\mathbb{R}\times(0,\infty):\omega(x_{0}-z)\geq h\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})

\displaystyle-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)\leq\inf\bigl\{\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr):(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\inf\bigl\{\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{h}(z)\bigr)+|\psi_{0}(z-\omega h)-\psi_{0}(x_{0})|:(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})\bigr\}.

(104)

We now bound
\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{h}(z)\bigr)
for
(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})
. Recalling that
c_{n,\delta}:=(1+4\epsilon_{n,\delta})(1-2/n)
, we apply (
100
) in Lemma
24
and the fact that
\alpha_{z,h}\leq 1/4
to obtain

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(1)}(z)\geq\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)

\displaystyle\geq c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)\biggl(1-\Pi_{1,z,h}\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\Bigr\}\biggr)\geq\frac{c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)}{2}>0.

(105)

If
\psi_{h}(z)=0
, then invoking Lemma
22
and combining (
105
) with (
101
) in Lemma
24
yields

\displaystyle 0\leq\omega\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)\leq\frac{\omega\hat{\tau}\,\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)}\leq\frac{2\Pi_{2,z,h}}{h}\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\biggr\}\leq\frac{\beta_{z,h}}{2},

(106)

where
\hat{\tau}\equiv\hat{\tau}_{n,h}(z)
. On the other hand, if
\psi_{h}(z)\neq 0
, then for some
\omega^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}
, it follows from Lemma
22
, (
105
) and Lemma
24
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\omega\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega)}(z)-\psi_{h}(z)\bigr)=\omega\psi_{h}(z)\biggl\{\frac{\hat{\tau}\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)/\bigl(c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f^{\prime}_{h}(z)\bigr)}{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega^{\prime})}(z)/\bigl(c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)\bigr)}-1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\omega\psi_{h}(z)\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(-1)}(z)}{c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\hat{\tau}\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f^{\prime}_{h}(z)}-1\biggr)+\biggl(1-\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega^{\prime})}(z)}{c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,|\psi_{h}(z)|\,\biggl\{\omega\biggl(\frac{\hat{\tau}\hat{M}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega\hat{\tau})}(z)}{|c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f^{\prime}_{h}(z)|}-\sgn f_{h}^{\prime}(z)\biggr)+\Bigl|1-\frac{\hat{D}_{n,\delta,h}^{(\omega^{\prime})}(z)}{c_{n,\delta}h^{2}f_{h}(z)}\Bigr|\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,|\psi_{h}(z)|\,\biggl(\frac{\Pi_{2,z,h}}{h|\psi_{h}(z)|}+\Pi_{1,z,h}\biggr)\biggl\{\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\vee\frac{\epsilon_{n,\delta}}{hf_{h}(z)}\biggr\}\leq\beta_{z,h}

(107)

Since
(z,h)\in\mathcal{H}_{f_{0}}^{(\omega)}(x_{0})
was arbitrary, combining (
107
) with (
106
) and (
104
) yields the result.

(b)
By (
99
) and the definition of
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
, we have
\psi_{0}(x_{0}),\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
, so

\displaystyle\bigl|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr|\leq\lambda\bigl(\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigr)=\bigl(\psi_{0}(x_{0})-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(-1)}(x_{0})\bigr)+\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}^{(1)}(x_{0})-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)

and the result follows from part
(a)
.

(c)
Since
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
is defined in (
18
) as the Euclidean projection of
0
onto
\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
, we have
\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigl(y-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigr)\geq 0
for all
y\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
. Moreover,
\psi_{0}(x_{0})\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})
by (
99
), so considering
y=\psi_{0}(x_{0})
yields

\displaystyle\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})-\psi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\psi_{0}(x_{0})-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigr)^{2}+\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle=\psi_{0}(x_{0})^{2}-2\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigl(\psi_{0}(x_{0})-\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})\bigr)\leq\psi_{0}(x_{0})^{2}

and hence
|\hat{\psi}_{n,\delta}(x_{0})-\psi_{0}(x_{0})|\leq|\psi_{0}(x_{0})|
.
∎

Appendix C
Auxiliary results

Lemma 25
.

Let
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be a closed interval. Then an upper semicontinuous log-concave density
f_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
is continuous on
I
if and only if
f_{0}
is absolutely continuous on
I
.

Proof.

Let
a:=\inf\supp f_{0}\equiv F_{0}^{-1}(0)
and
b:=\sup\supp f_{0}\equiv F_{0}^{-1}(1)
, which are the two points at which
f_{0}
may be discontinuous (if they are finite). Since
f_{0}
is log-concave, we have
f_{0}(\pm\infty):=\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}f_{0}(x)=0

(
18
, Lemma 1)
, and
J_{0}=f_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
is non-negative and concave on
[0,1]

(
9
, Proposition A.1(c))
, with
J_{0}(0)=f_{0}(a)
and
J_{0}(1)=b
. Therefore, by
59
,
J_{0}
is absolutely continuous on
[0,1]
. Moreover,
\|{f_{0}}\|_{\infty}=\|{J_{0}}\|_{\infty}<\infty
, so
F_{0}
is Lipschitz on
\mathbb{R}
. It follows that
f_{0}=J_{0}\circ F_{0}
is absolutely continuous on
[a,b]
. If
f_{0}
is also continuous at either
a
or
b
, then
f_{0}
is absolutely continuous on
(-\infty,b]
or
[a,\infty)
respectively.
∎

The proofs of our upper bounds over the classes
\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}
in Section
2.3
rely on the following envelope functions for the associated density quantile functions and their derivatives.

Lemma 26
.

For
\beta\in[1,2]
,
L>0
and
u\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}}|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq 2L^{1/\beta}\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u\wedge(1-u)}\Bigr),

(108)

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}}|J_{0}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq 4L^{1/\beta}\bigl(u\wedge(1-u)\bigr)\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u\wedge(1-u)}\Bigr),

(109)

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta,L}}i(f_{0})

\displaystyle\leq 8L^{2/\beta},

(110)

where we can take
C_{\beta}:=\sqrt{2\beta^{1/(\beta-1)}(\beta-1)/\pi}\leq 2/\sqrt{\pi}
.

Proof.

If
\beta=1
, then
|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|=|(\psi_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1})(u)|\leq L
for all
u\in(0,1)
, so by Lemma
14
(b)
,
i(f_{0})=\int_{0}^{1}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}\leq L^{2}
. Moreover,
J_{0}(0)=J_{0}(1)=0
by Lemma
14
(a)
, so
|J_{0}(u)|\leq\int_{0}^{u}|J_{0}^{\prime}|\wedge\int_{u}^{1}|J_{0}^{\prime}|\leq L\bigl(u\wedge(1-u)\bigr)
for all
u\in[0,1]
, so the result holds.

Next, consider
\beta\in(1,2]
and fix
u\in(0,1)
. Let
x:=F_{0}^{-1}(u)
and
\rho:=\psi_{0}(x)=J_{0}^{\prime}(u)
. Since
\phi_{0}:=\log f_{0}
is concave,

\phi_{0}(y)\leq\phi_{0}(x)+\rho(y-x)

for all
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, if
y\geq x
, then by the Hölder condition,

\phi_{0}(y)-\phi_{0}(x)=\int_{x}^{y}\psi_{0}\geq\int_{x}^{y}\bigl(\rho-L(z-x)^{\beta-1}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}z=\rho(y-x)-\frac{L}{\beta}(y-x)^{\beta}.

Suppose first that
\rho>0
and let
t:=(\rho^{\beta}\beta/L)^{1/(\beta-1)}
. Then by the change of variables
z=\rho(y-x)/t
, we have

\frac{u}{1-u}=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\phi_{0}}}{\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{\phi_{0}}}\leq\frac{f_{0}(x)\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\rho(y-x)}\,\mathrm{d}y}{f_{0}(x)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{\rho(y-x)-L(y-x)^{\beta}/\beta}\,\mathrm{d}y}=\frac{1/\rho}{(t/\rho)\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{t(z-z^{\beta})}\,\mathrm{d}z}.

(111)

The function
z\mapsto z-z^{\beta}=:\zeta(z)
on
(0,\infty)
is maximised by
z_{*}:=\beta^{-1/(\beta-1)}
, with
\zeta(z_{*})=(\beta-1)/\beta^{\beta/(\beta-1)}
. If
w\geq 0
, then by Taylor’s theorem with the mean value form of the remainder, there exists
\bar{w}\in[0,w]
such that

\displaystyle\zeta(z_{*}+w)-\zeta(z_{*})=w\zeta^{\prime}(z_{*})+\frac{w^{2}}{2}\zeta^{\prime\prime}(z_{*}+\bar{w})=-\frac{\beta(\beta-1)w^{2}}{2(z_{*}+\bar{w})^{2-\beta}}\geq-\frac{\beta(\beta-1)w^{2}}{2z_{*}^{2-\beta}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{t(z-z^{\beta})}\,\mathrm{d}z\geq\int_{z_{*}}^{\infty}e^{t\zeta(z)}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq e^{t\zeta(z_{*})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t\beta(\beta-1)z_{*}^{\beta-2}w^{2}}{2}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}w=\frac{e^{t\zeta(z_{*})}}{C_{\beta}t^{1/2}},

so by (
111
),

\frac{t^{1/2}e^{t\zeta(z_{*})}}{C_{\beta}}\leq t\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{t(z-z^{\beta})}\leq\frac{1-u}{u}.

Thus, when
\rho>0
, we have

\Bigl(\frac{\rho^{\beta}\beta}{L}\Bigr)^{1/(\beta-1)}=t\leq\max\biggl\{\frac{1}{\zeta(z_{*})}\log\biggl(\frac{C_{\beta}(1-u)}{u}\biggr),\,1\biggr\},

so

\rho\leq\Bigl(\frac{L}{\beta}\Bigr)^{1/\beta}\max\biggl\{\frac{\beta}{(\beta-1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}}\log^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\biggl(\frac{C_{\beta}(1-u)}{u}\biggr),\,1\biggr\}.

Otherwise, if
\rho<0
, then by applying the above reasoning to the log-concave density
x\mapsto f_{0}(-x)
instead, we obtain

\rho\geq-\Bigl(\frac{L}{\beta}\Bigr)^{1/\beta}\max\biggl\{\frac{\beta}{(\beta-1)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}}\log^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\biggl(\frac{C_{\beta}u}{1-u}\biggr),\,1\biggr\}.

Therefore, in all cases,

|J_{0}^{\prime}(u)|=|\rho|\leq 2L^{1/\beta}\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u\wedge(1-u)}\Bigr)

for every
u\in(0,1)
, which establishes (
108
). If
u\in(0,1/2]
, then since
J_{0}(0)=0
, integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\frac{J_{0}(u)}{2L^{1/\beta}}\leq\int_{0}^{u}\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{v}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\Bigl[v\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{v}\Bigr)\Bigr]_{0}^{u}+\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{u\wedge(C_{\beta}/e)}\log^{-1/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{v}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq u\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u}\Bigr)+\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}\Bigl(u\wedge\frac{C_{\beta}}{e}\Bigr)\log_{+}^{-1/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\beta-1}{\beta}u\log_{+}^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{u}\Bigr).

Similarly, for
u\in(1/2,1)
, this bound holds with
u
replaced with
1-u
, so (
109
) follows. Finally, by Lemma
14
(b)
and integrating by parts as above,

\displaystyle\frac{i(f_{0})}{L^{2/\beta}}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}}{L^{2/\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\times 4\int_{0}^{1/2}\log_{+}^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{v}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=8\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\log_{+}^{2(\beta-1)/\beta}(2C_{\beta})+\frac{2(\beta-1)}{\beta}\int_{0}^{C_{\beta}/e}\log^{(\beta-2)/\beta}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\beta}}{v}\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 8\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{e\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigr)<8,

(112)

where the penultimate and final lines rely on
\beta\leq 2
and
C_{\beta}\leq 2/\sqrt{\pi}<e/2
, so (
110
) holds.
∎

The remaining results establish some fundamental properties of the
\mathrm{kl}
function defined in (
16
), which is central to the multiscale construction in Section
3
.

Lemma 27
.

Suppose that
Y_{1},\dotsc,Y_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathrm{Bern}(p)
for some
p\in(0,1)
and let
\bar{Y}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}
. Then for every
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)\leq e^{-n\varepsilon}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)\leq 2e^{-n\varepsilon}.

Proof.

The function
\eta\mapsto\mathrm{kl}(p+\eta,p)
is continuous and strictly increasing on
[0,1-p)
with
\lim_{\eta\nearrow 1-p}\mathrm{kl}(p+\eta,p)=\log(1/p)
. Thus, if
\varepsilon>\log(1/p)
, then

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)=0\leq e^{-n\varepsilon}.

On the other hand, if
\varepsilon\in\bigl(0,\log(1/p)\bigr]
, then there exists a unique
\eta_{0}\in[0,1-p)
such that
\mathrm{kl}(p+\eta_{0},p)=\varepsilon

(
58
)
, so by a version of Hoeffding’s inequality
(e.g.
61
, Exercise 10.6.7
(b)
)
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)=\mathbb{P}(\bar{Y}-p\geq\eta_{0})\leq e^{-n\mathrm{kl}(p+\eta_{0},p)}=e^{-n\varepsilon}.

Similarly, by replacing
p
and
\bar{Y}
with
1-p
and
1-\bar{Y}
respectively, we obtain

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(1-\bar{Y},1-p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)\leq e^{-n\varepsilon},

so

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(\bar{Y},p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)+\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}_{+}(1-\bar{Y},1-p)\geq\varepsilon\bigr)\leq 2e^{-n\varepsilon},

as required.
∎

For two probability measures
P
and
Q
on a measurable space
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})
, let

\mathrm{KL}(P,Q):=\begin{cases}\log\bigl(\frac{dP}{dQ}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}P&\text{if }P\ll Q\\
\infty&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

denote the Kullback–Leibler divergence from
Q
to
P
.

Lemma 28
.

The function
\mathrm{kl}:[0,1]\times[0,1]\to[0,\infty]
is jointly convex. Hence, for any Borel measurable functions
p,q:[0,1]\to[0,1]
,

\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}p(\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma,\int_{0}^{1}q(\gamma)\,\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\biggr)\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{kl}\bigl(p(\gamma),q(\gamma)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma.

Proof.

Let
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[0,1]
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
. For
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, let
P_{\ell}:=\mathrm{Bern}(p_{\ell})
and
Q_{\ell}:=\mathrm{Bern}(q_{\ell})
. Then
\lambda P_{1}+(1-\lambda)P_{2}
is a Bernoulli distribution with parameter
\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}
, so

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2},\lambda q_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}\bigl(\lambda P_{1}+(1-\lambda)P_{2},\lambda Q_{1}+(1-\lambda)Q_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},Q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{KL}(P_{2},Q_{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathrm{kl}(p_{1},q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}(p_{2},q_{2}),

where we have used joint convexity of the Kullback–Leibler divergence
(
61
, Exercise 8.7)
. Thus,
\mathrm{kl}:[0,1]\times[0,1]\to[0,\infty]
is jointly convex.

For the second claim, let
\Gamma\sim\text{Unif}(0,1)
, let
X_{1}:=p(\Gamma)
and let
X_{2}:=q(\Gamma)
. Applying the multivariate version of Jensen’s inequality
(
61
, Theorem 10.3.11)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}p(\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma,\int_{0}^{1}q(\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\biggr)=\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(X_{1}),\mathbb{E}(X_{2})\bigr)\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathrm{kl}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigr)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{kl}\bigl(p(\gamma),q(\gamma)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma,

as required.
∎

Lemma 29
.

The function
\mathrm{kl}_{+}:[0,1]\times[0,1]\to[0,\infty]
is jointly convex.

Proof.

Let
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[0,1]
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Define
p:=\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}
and
q:=\lambda q_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}
. If
p\leq q
, then

\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p,q)=0\leq\lambda\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{1},q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{2},q_{2}).

Now suppose that
p>q
. If both
p_{1}>q_{1}
and
p_{2}>q_{2}
, then by Lemma
28
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p,q)=\mathrm{kl}(p,q)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\mathrm{kl}(p_{1},q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}(p_{2},q_{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{1},q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{2},q_{2}).

If
p_{1}\leq q_{1}
, then we must have
p_{2}>q_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}\leq\lambda q_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}=q<p.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p,q)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{kl}_{+}\bigl(\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2},\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2},\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)q_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}(p_{2},q_{2})=\lambda\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{1},q_{1})+(1-\lambda)\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p_{2},q_{2}),

where we have used the fact that
\mathrm{kl}_{+}(p^{\prime},\cdot)
is decreasing on
[0,1]
for each
p^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, as well as Lemma
28
. The case where
p_{2}\leq q_{2}
follows similarly.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Given any
p,q\in[0,1]^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{9(p-q)^{2}}{2(p+2q)(3-p-2q)}\leq\mathrm{kl}(p,q).

Consequently, for
\eta>0
, if
\mathrm{kl}(p,q)<9\eta/2
then

\Bigl|p-\frac{2(1-2\eta)q+3\eta}{2(1+\eta)}\Bigr|\leq\frac{3\sqrt{4q(1-q)\eta+\eta^{2}}}{2(1+\eta)}

and

\Bigl|q-\frac{(1-2\eta)p+3\eta}{1+4\eta}\Bigr|\leq\frac{3\sqrt{p(1-p)\eta+\eta^{2}}}{1+4\eta}.

Proof.

For the first bound, see for example
58
. Given
\eta>0
, rearrangement of the resulting inequality yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq p^{2}(1+\eta)-\bigl(2(1-2\eta)q+3\eta\bigr)p+(1+4\eta)q^{2}-6q\eta,

which is equivalent to both the second and third bounds.
∎
